## 2026-11-06 · M3

### Warm-up check kept and made precise (AKS-129)

- **Decision:** spec 2's paired-halves warm-up check stays, with its 99% level and 5% threshold, in preference to MSER-5 and Welch's method (`docs/research/spec-2-output-analysis.md`). Spec 2 "Statistic definitions" now states it precisely for the experiment manager (AKS-102). Simplest readings of the proposal, each marked owner to confirm in spec 2:
  - It reads the `timeseries` samples as they are (a sample mean), not a time-weighted integral of the path, and the observed samples are those with `time_s` at or after `warmup`, the boundary every other statistic uses.
  - The halves are by sample count: the first and last ⌊J/2⌋ of the J observed samples, the middle one dropped when J is odd; a metric with J < 2 is skipped.
  - The "mean" in the 5% threshold is the mean over replications of each replication's mean of all J observed samples; both comparisons are strict, so a constant series never fires.
  - The level is 99% whatever the experiment's `confidence` (the proposal fixed 99%; `confidence` sets the reported intervals).
  - One W210 per run, not one per metric, listing each changed metric with its scenario and the change as a whole percentage of its mean, `component` null, `path` `/experiments/<index>/warmup`, hint "Lengthen 'warmup' and run again.". One issue keeps `summary.json` short when several series drift together (a server's queue length and `model.wip` move as one), and matches spec 4's one `not_enough_runs` finding per run.
  - Only a plain experiment is checked; a search's candidates are decided at M5, where the search layout is built.
- **Reason:** guide rules 3, 5, 6 and 7; spec 2 "Research before building" (M3) and the warm-up bullet's "may replace it with MSER-5 or Welch and must say why". The check tests the question W210 asks with a false-alarm rate set by its level, since replications are independent; MSER-5 estimates a truncation point with no significance level and, on M2 output, returned non-zero points on the shipped warehouse and three-machine line; Welch's method needs a person to read a plot. Tested on the M2 adapter (the note's "Tested"): the coffee shop is silent at seed 12345 and at 40 of 40 other seeds; a slowly filling model with no warm-up fires at every seed; at 99% the shipped warehouse fires at 0 of 40 seeds and the line at 2 of 40 (utilization changes of 5.6% and 7.0% at seeds 33 and 24, false alarms on a stationary station).
- **Alternatives considered:**
  - MSER-5 as the trigger (fire when its truncation point exceeds the warm-up, or exceeds a fraction of the series): no published threshold; noisy on the shipped examples.
  - Welch's method: graphical, not automatable.
  - The same check at 95%: it fires more often on a server at utilization 0.95 with no warm-up over 20 h (33 of 40 seeds against 24 of 40 at 99%), but also on 2 to 4 of 40 correct runs of each shipped example.
  - One W210 per metric: more precise but repetitive; the message lists the metrics instead.
- **Known limit:** the check fires only on bias the 30 replications can tell apart from their noise. A single server at utilization 0.95 with no warm-up fires at 24 of 40 seeds over 20 h (not at seed 12345) and at 0 of 40 over 200 h, where a 50 h warm-up leaves the estimate unchanged, so the shortfall there is imprecision rather than warm-up; spec 4's `not_enough_runs` fires on the server's wait in both (half-width 32.5% and 11.8% of the mean). Recorded in the note; the 99% level and 5% threshold are revisited with the finding thresholds at M8 and M10.
- **Spec sections updated:** spec 2 "Statistic definitions" (the warm-up bullet) and "Changes in this version" › "Since version 0.5". Spec 1's W210 row ("Results were still changing after warm-up; lengthen `warmup`") still fits and is unchanged.
- **Dependencies added:** none.

### Experiment manager: across-replication statistics, scenarios, warm-up check, summary and tables (AKS-102)

- **Decision:**
  - **Where it lives.** `src/lcsim/runs/`: `statistics.py` (the mean and half-width across replications, `estimate`, `across_replications`, and `paired_differences`), `warmup.py` (the AKS-129 warm-up check, `warmup_check`, and its W210, `warmup_warning`), `summary.py` (`build_summary`, `fidelity_label`, `format_length`), `tables.py` (`write_summary`, `write_tables`) and `manager.py` (`run_experiment`, which takes an adapter through `validate`, `translate`, `run` and `collect` in a given workdir, and `fidelity_issues`, E173). Nothing in it knows the run folder, the manifest or job states, which AKS-103 builds on top.
  - **The half-width** is t(1 − (1 − confidence)/2, n − 1) × s / √n with t from `scipy.stats.t.ppf`, as spec 2 says; the example test (`tests/engines/process/test_example_models.py`) now uses the manager's `estimate` instead of its own copy, and its three t-quantile checks moved to `tests/runs/test_run_statistics.py`, with a hand computation on two small fixtures.
  - **NaN replication values (rule 5, owner to confirm).** A NaN value is left out; the mean and half-width are over the n values that remain, with t at n − 1 degrees of freedom; `summary.json` reports `n` with every metric; with n = 1 the half-width is `null`, and with n = 0 the mean too. This is the first of the two simplest readings the ticket offered. It keeps a metric that is undefined in a few replications (a wait in a replication where no service started after warm-up) usable, and `n` shows the reader how many replications it rests on. Spec 2 "Statistic definitions".
  - **Paired scenario differences in `summary.json` (rule 5, owner to confirm).** A top-level `differences` list, one entry per pair of scenarios in the experiment's order (first with second, first with third, …, then second with third), `{"scenario": <later>, "against": <earlier>, "metrics": {<metric>: {mean, half_width, unit, n, separated}}}`, the difference being `scenario` minus `against`; empty for one scenario. A replication is paired only when both values are not NaN. `separated` is n ≥ 2 and |mean| > half-width, strict, so an interval touching zero is not separated. Spec 1 "Run folder and results" and spec 2 "Statistic definitions".
  - **Other `summary.json` readings (rule 5, owner to confirm), spec 1 "Run folder and results":**
    - Key order `model`, `experiment`, `fidelity`, `fidelity_label`, `replications`, `confidence`, `scenarios`, `differences`, `warnings`, as in the spec 1 example; metrics in the `replications` table's row order; scenarios in the experiment's order.
    - A scenario's `set` is in SI units (a `quantity` parameter as seconds), since results are stored in SI.
    - `warnings` lists the adapter's `validate` warnings, then `collect`'s, then the manager's W210.
    - `fidelity_label` is "<fidelity, capitalised>: <replications> replications of <length>, <confidence × 100>% confidence"; the length is a whole number in the largest Phase 1 unit that gives one (`d`, `h`, `min`), else seconds ("200 h"; 48 h is "2 d"); the percentage drops a trailing `.0`.
    - An optimization run's summary is `model`, then `Results.solution`, then `fidelity_label` ("Standard") and `warnings`; no statistics (spec 1 "Division of labour"). Not run until M5.
  - **Warm-up check readings beyond AKS-129 (rule 5, owner to confirm), spec 2 "Statistic definitions":**
    - A metric whose m̄ is 0 is not reported as changed: its change as a percentage of its mean is undefined. Every Phase 1 series is at least 0, so m̄ = 0 only when every sample is 0, and then d̄ = 0 anyway.
    - p rounds a half up (`floor(x + 0.5)`), not to even as Python's `round` does.
    - A metric with fewer than 2 replications is not checked (E170 makes this unreachable for a valid model); replications with different observed sample counts are an adapter fault and raise `ValueError`.
    - A search experiment is not checked (AKS-129: M5), and `run_experiment` refuses one with `NotImplementedError` naming M5.
  - **E173.** `run_experiment` refuses a `run.fidelity` other than `standard` with E173 at `/run/fidelity`, before anything is translated, since the fidelity label depends on it (spec 1 "Top-level keys": Phase 1 engines accept only `standard`; E173 is a run code the manager may raise). Validate errors refuse the run the same way.
  - **Tables.** `write_tables` writes `tables/replications.parquet` and `tables/timeseries.parquet` only (the names spec 1 lists), requires a `unit` column and drops any schema metadata, so units are a column and never Parquet metadata. `write_summary` writes UTF-8 JSON and refuses NaN, which JSON cannot hold. Both write through a temporary file and a rename.
  - **Carry-overs.** `CURRENT_MILESTONE` in `tests/benchmarks/conftest.py` is `M3`; no M3 benchmark exists, so the five M2 cases run as before (`pytest -m benchmark`: 5 passed). The benchmark harness keeps its own 3-standard-error arithmetic, which is the pass rule of spec 2 "Benchmarks", not a half-width.
- **Reason:** guide rules 4 to 7; spec 1 "Division of labour" ("the manager computes means, half-widths and paired scenario differences, writes `tables/*.parquet`, `summary.json`"), "Run folder and results" and "Tables for the `process` domain"; spec 2 "Experiment manager" and "Statistic definitions"; `docs/research/spec-2-output-analysis.md`; the AKS-102 acceptance list. Formulas: the Student t interval for a mean and for paired differences (NIST/SEMATECH e-Handbook of Statistical Methods, section 7.2.2.1), and the AKS-129 paired-halves check.
- **Alternatives considered:**
  - NaN rule: report the metric's mean and half-width as `null` whenever any replication is NaN, or always use all n replications. The first throws away good replications; the second has no value to use.
  - Differences: a mapping keyed by `"<later>-<earlier>"` (a key to parse), or nested under each scenario (a pair belongs to neither); every ordered pair both ways (redundant: one is the negative of the other).
  - `n` only where it differs from `replications`: a key that comes and goes is harder to read than one always there.
  - `fidelity_label` with the length as the file wrote it: the model keeps only SI values (guide "Units"), so the text is rebuilt from seconds.
- **Tested:** `tests/runs/` (74 tests): `test_run_statistics.py` (the half-width against `scipy.stats.t.ppf` and published quantiles, NaN values, undefined values, `separated`, the pairing), `test_warmup.py` (the statistic against a hand computation, the warm-up boundary, time order, both thresholds, a constant series, m̄ = 0, J < 2, the message and path, rounding; the research note's slow fill on the process adapter with 5 replications of 100 h sampled every 2 h, seed 12345: warm-up 0 fires with d̄ +66.0 on m̄ 266.7 and half-width 35.4, "changed by 25%"; warm-up 50 h is silent, d̄ +10.9 against half-width 51.7), `test_summary.py`, `test_tables.py`, `test_manager.py`. `tests/engines/process/test_example_models.py` runs the three examples through `run_experiment` and checks that none fires W210 at seed 12345 (the coffee shop is the acceptance case) and that the summary's means and half-widths are the manager's. `bash scripts/check.sh` exits 0.
- **Spec sections updated:** spec 1 "Run folder and results" (the example gains `n` and `differences`; a list of how the manager fills `summary.json`) and "Changes in this version" › new "Since version 0.5"; spec 2 "Statistic definitions" (the NaN rule, the paired differences made precise, the warm-up m̄ = 0 case, rounding and the place of W210) and "Changes in this version" › "Since version 0.5".
- **Dependencies added:** none new. `scipy>=1.17.1` moves from `[project.optional-dependencies] dev` to `[project] dependencies`, since the manager calls `scipy.stats.t.ppf` for every run. Its licence row is in "Libraries for M2, confirmed from their licence files" (BSD-3-Clause with the bundled libraries listed there; compatible with AGPL-3.0); re-checked on 2026-11-06 from the installed `scipy-1.18.1.dist-info/LICENSE.txt` on Python 3.13.18 (BSD 3-clause). The lower bound 1.17.1 is the newest scipy for Python 3.11 (1.18.1 needs 3.12), as recorded at AKS-101. pyarrow (`pyarrow>=26.0.1`, Apache-2.0) is already a runtime dependency, recorded in the same table.

### Local run target, run folder, manifest, job states, cancel and limits (AKS-103)

- **Decision:**
  - **Where it lives.** `src/lcsim/runs/`: `targets.py` (`LocalTarget`, `LocalRun`, `RunRefused`, `recover_interrupted`), `worker.py` (`child_main`, the spawned worker's body, and `python -m lcsim.runs.worker`), `manifest.py`, `states.py`, `ids.py`, `limits.py` and `lock.py`. `manager.run_experiment` is split into `check_experiment` (validate and E173) and `collect_summary` (collect and the summary), which the target calls in its own process; `run_experiment` keeps its behaviour and AKS-102's tests pass unchanged.
  - **Where each method runs (rule 5, owner to confirm).** Spec 1 "Run targets" says `validate`, `translate` and `collect` run in "the service's worker process" and `run` "in the worker process". Reading: the first three run in the process that drives the run (the caller of `create` and `execute`; the service's run worker from M6), and `run` runs in a separate worker process started with `multiprocessing`'s `spawn` method, which creates the adapter's class with no arguments (the class is passed by reference, so an adapter keeps no state between methods; spec 1 already says adapters are created with no arguments). The worker is not a daemon, so the process adapter can start its own pool.
  - **Refused before a run exists (rule 5, owner to confirm).** In order: the file is loaded (its error issues refuse); a `run.target` other than `"local"`, including a non-text value, is E172 at `/run/target`; the adapter is chosen (E102, which loading has already checked against the same engines); the adapter's `validate` and the fidelity check (E173). Any error raises `RunRefused(issues)` and creates nothing (no `run_id`, no folder): spec 1 says E172 never reaches a manifest, and spec 3 returns 422 for validation errors, so the same applies to every check. A search experiment and an optimization model raise `NotImplementedError` naming M5.
  - **`model.json` (rule 5, owner to confirm)** is the model file's bytes exactly as given to `create`, not a canonical re-serialisation: the loaded model keeps only SI values and no text layout, so a dump would not be "the model that ran" as the user wrote it, and the file's bytes are byte-equal on a repeat from the same file (AKS-104). A seed the file leaves out is chosen at load and recorded only in the manifest's `seed`.
  - **`run_id` randomness.** The six base32 characters come from `secrets.token_bytes` (the operating system's generator), RFC 4648 upper-case alphabet. They are an identifier, not simulation randomness, so the spec 1 stream scheme does not apply and this is not a breach of the guide's "Randomness" rule; neither `random` nor `numpy.random` is used. A clash with an existing folder draws again.
  - **Manifest values (rule 5, owner to confirm).** `fraction` is the run's progress (spec 3 progress events and spec 4 `get_run_status` both return `fraction`): 0 when queued, each `Progress.fraction` the adapter reports, 1 at `done`; a cancelled or failed run keeps its last value. The progress `message` is not written (spec 1 lists no such field; the service keeps it in memory at M6). On progress the manifest is written for the first report and then at most once a second; state changes are written at once and do not delay the next progress write. `project_id` is `null` and `client` is `script` until the service passes them at M6; `host` is `socket.gethostname()`; `started` and `ended` are `null` until reached; times are ISO 8601 UTC to the millisecond ending in `Z`; `error` is present only on a failed run; `data_files` stays `[]` until M6. Writes go to a temporary file in the run folder, then `os.replace`.
  - **`queued` to `failed` (rule 5, owner to confirm).** Spec 1 lists the allowed moves and says "No other moves", but also says the service marks runs whose manifest says `queued` or `running` and whose worker is gone as `failed` with E191. The two cannot both hold for a queued run, so the move `queued` to `failed` is added, used only by E191. This departs from the move list in the AKS-103 ticket, for that reason; every other move is refused (`StateError`, tested over all 25 pairs).
  - **How E191 knows a worker is not alive (rule 5, owner to confirm).** The process driving a run holds an exclusive lock on `worker.lock` in the run folder from creation to the final state, then removes the file. `recover_interrupted(runs_root)` (to be called by the service on start) tries the lock of each run whose manifest says `queued` or `running`; when it can be taken, or the file is gone, the run is failed with E191. A lock is released by the operating system when its process ends, however it ends, and cannot be inherited by a reused process id, unlike a recorded pid; checking a pid platform-neutrally would also need `psutil` or `ctypes` (on Windows `os.kill(pid, 0)` terminates the process). `fcntl.flock` on Linux and macOS, `msvcrt.locking` on Windows, both standard library and imported only when used. The worker process also watches its driver (`multiprocessing.parent_process().is_alive()`), so its token reads as set and it stops when the driver ends.
  - **Cancel.** `LocalRun.cancel()` sets the `multiprocessing.Event`. A queued run moves to `cancelled` at once and is never started. A running worker that has not stopped 5 s after cancel is terminated (`Process.terminate()`). A run whose token was set ends `cancelled`, even if the adapter had just finished, and nothing is collected (as `run_experiment`).
  - **E190.** An exception from `translate`, `run` (reported by the worker with its traceback) or `collect` (including writing the tables and summary), or a worker that ends without reporting, fails the run with E190, `path` `""`, `component` `null`, a message naming the engine and a hint pointing to `log.txt`; the traceback or exit status goes to `log.txt` only.
  - **E192 (rule 5, owner to confirm).** Limits are parameters of `LocalTarget` (`Limits(time_s=600, memory_bytes=half of physical memory)`, spec 4 "Limits"). The time limit counts from `started` and is checked every 0.1 s while the worker runs. Memory is the resident memory of the worker and every process it started, read from `/proc/<pid>/statm` and `/proc/*/stat` once a second, because the process adapter's work happens in pool processes that a per-process limit would miss and resident memory is what "half of system memory" is about. On either limit the token is set, the worker has 2 s to stop, then it is terminated, and the run fails with E192.
  - **Known limit: memory on Windows and macOS.** They have no `/proc`, and Windows has no `os.sysconf`, so there the memory limit is not enforced and `log.txt` says so on each run; the time limit is enforced everywhere. A Windows Job Object or `psutil` would close the gap; decided with packaging at M11 or when the owner answers AKS-77. `resource.setrlimit` was not used: it limits address space rather than resident memory, applies per process, and does not exist on Windows.
  - **Load-time warnings in `summary.json` (rule 5, owner to confirm; the point AKS-102 left open).** The target passes the warnings found at load (W200 to W202) to `build_summary` first, before the adapter's `validate` warnings. A user who reads only the results, or an assistant reading `summary.json` through MCP, should still see that a queue grows without limit (W201) or that samples are clipped (W202); these say the numbers may not mean what they seem, which is what the summary's warnings are for.
  - **The worker entry point (rule 5, owner to confirm).** `python -m lcsim.runs.worker <workdir> [--workers N]`: reads the adapter's name from `engine.name` in `<workdir>/../manifest.json` (the whole run folder is synced to the host, spec 1 "Run targets"), creates that installed adapter, and calls `run` with `N` workers (default 1; the remote host's tier is not known here). The token is set once `<workdir>/CANCEL` exists, looked at no more than every 0.05 s because the process adapter asks often. One JSON line per progress report on standard output, in the spec 3 progress-event shape; exit status 0 done, 1 adapter error (traceback on standard error), 2 unusable arguments, manifest or adapter, 3 cancelled. It writes nothing itself and enforces no limits (the starting side does, from M11). It has the `if __name__ == "__main__":` guard.
  - **Platform-neutral code (spec 3 "Architecture"; AKS-77 still open).** `spawn` only, no `os.fork`, no signals (only `Process.terminate()`, which is `TerminateProcess` on Windows), no Linux-only paths except the guarded `/proc` reading, and the lock chosen by `os.name`. Not run on Windows in this session: the Windows lock is tested against a stand-in `msvcrt` module.
  - **Coverage.** `[tool.coverage.report] exclude_also` excludes the `if __name__ == "__main__":` block, the only line that runs only as a script; `main()` is tested in this process and the module as a real subprocess. Code in the spawned worker (`child_main`) is also called in this process by a test, so its lines are measured. No `# pragma: no cover` is used.
- **Reason:** guide rules 4 to 7 and "Conventions" (Files, Errors, Randomness); spec 1 "Run targets", "Engine adapter interface", "Run folder and results" and "Validation issues" (E172, E190, E191, E192); spec 3 "Architecture" and "Service rules"; spec 4 "Limits"; the AKS-103 acceptance list.
- **Alternatives considered:**
  - Run all five methods in the worker process: simpler, but spec 1 places `validate`, `translate` and `collect` on this machine for every target, which keeps the remote and cluster targets the same at M11.
  - Pass the adapter instance to the worker by pickling: an adapter's state would then differ between processes without saying so; creating the class with no arguments matches discovery.
  - A pid file for E191: pids are reused, and checking a pid portably needs `psutil` or `ctypes`.
  - Mark queued orphans `cancelled` instead of `failed`: contradicts E191's wording.
  - `model.json` as a canonical dump: not possible from the loaded model without a writer, and it would not be the file the user wrote.
  - `resource.RLIMIT_AS` in the worker for memory: per process, counts address space, POSIX only.
  - A `worker.json` file in `engine/` for the worker's adapter name: one more file, where the manifest already names the engine.
- **Tested:** `tests/runs/test_dummy_adapter.py` (spec 1 acceptance, M3: the dummy's `capabilities` and `validate` at `create`, `translate` and `collect` in this process, `run` in another process after `translate`, each once); `test_local_target.py` (the full run folder and every manifest field, `model.json` byte-equal to an oddly formatted file, a chosen seed in the manifest, a real `workers=2` run of the coffee shop with the process adapter and a listing of every new path showing nothing outside `engine/` but the manager's files, E172 for `remote`, `cluster` and a non-text target with no folder, load errors, E300 from `validate`, E173, E102, M5 cases, cancel while queued, during `translate` and while running (state `cancelled` within 1.5 s of cancel; measured about 0.3 s per test including the spawn), a worker ignoring cancel terminated, E190 from `run`, from a worker that exits without reporting and from `translate` and `collect`, E192 for a 0.3 s time limit and a 1-byte memory limit, refused moves, and E191 on fabricated orphan folders); `test_states.py`, `test_ids.py`, `test_manifest.py`, `test_lock.py` (including a real subprocess holding the lock and dying), `test_limits.py` and `test_worker.py` (in process, and `python -m lcsim.runs.worker` as a real subprocess with `--workers 2` and with `CANCEL` created mid-run: exit 3 within 2 s).
- **Review of PR #24 (Codex), three findings, each confirmed and fixed with a failing test first:**
  - A cancel accepted while `collect` ran was lost: the run ended `done` with results. Now the check of the token, the writing of `tables/` and `summary.json`, and the move to `done` happen while holding the run's mutex, which `cancel()` also takes, so a cancel either comes before the check (the run ends `cancelled`, nothing written) or finds the run final (and does nothing). Test: `test_cancel_during_collect_ends_cancelled_without_results`.
  - Terminating the worker left the processes it had started running (the process adapter's pool workers watch their own stop event, which a terminated worker never sets). Now, before terminating, the target reads the worker's descendants from `/proc` (`limits.descendant_ids`), and after terminating the worker it sends each of them SIGKILL. Known limit: on Windows and macOS there is no `/proc`, so only the worker is ended (a Windows Job Object would close this gap; decided with the memory limit's gap at M11 or AKS-77). Test: `test_terminating_a_worker_also_ends_the_processes_it_started` (a grandchild process ends within 5 s).
  - The watch loop stopped at pipe EOF and then waited for the worker without a limit, but a worker can close its pipe and then keep running (the interpreter waits for non-daemon threads before exit: `multiprocessing/process.py` calls `threading._shutdown()`). Now the loop keeps watching, with the limits and the cancel deadline applied, until the process has ended. Test: `test_a_worker_that_lingers_after_its_report_is_still_limited` (E192 within 10 s, not 30 s).
  - Second review (on 9c128fb): after `terminate()` the target waited for the worker without a limit, but on POSIX `terminate()` is SIGTERM, which a worker can ignore, so cancel and E192 could hang. Now it waits `kill_grace_s` (default 2 s) and then calls `Process.kill()` (SIGKILL on POSIX; the same as terminate on Windows) before the final join. Test: `test_a_worker_that_ignores_terminate_is_killed` (a worker that ignores SIGTERM ends `cancelled` within 5 s of cancel).
  - Third review (on a2f87de): each `cancel()` reset the cancel deadline, so repeated cancels less than 5 s apart postponed the forced stop. Now only the first cancel of a running run sets the deadline (and logs); later ones do nothing. Test: `test_cancelling_again_does_not_postpone_the_forced_stop` (cancel every 0.1 s with a 0.5 s grace; the run ends `cancelled` within 3 s).
  - Fourth review (on 57996b9): a worker process that could not be started (for example `OSError` at the process or file limit) raised out of `execute()` with the run left `running` and its lock held. Now a failed start closes both pipe ends and fails the run with E190, the traceback in `log.txt` (E190 because the engine's run could not happen; no other code fits, and adding one would change spec 1's list). Test: `test_a_worker_that_cannot_start_is_e190_and_the_run_ends`.
  - Also covered: a failure while writing the results (a table spec 1 does not name) is E190.
- **Spec sections updated:** spec 1 "Run targets" (the worker entry point), "Run folder and results" (the job-state moves and "How the `local` target runs a run"; the `warnings` bullet; after review, the cancel bullet and a new "Watching and stopping the worker" bullet) and "Changes in this version" › "Since version 0.5".
- **Dependencies added:** none (standard library only: `multiprocessing`, `secrets`, `fcntl`/`msvcrt`, `mmap`, `socket`).

### Identical repeat runs and cross-process reproducibility (AKS-104)

- **Decision:**
  - **The repeat test.** `tests/runs/test_repeat.py` runs the coffee shop (20 h after 2 h of warm-up, 3 replications, two scenarios so that `differences` is filled, written with an indent of 3 so that `model.json` is checked as the file's bytes) twice at seed 12345 on the local target, each `execute` in its own spawned worker, and finds them identical as spec 1 defines it: the `summary.json` metrics equal as parsed JSON (exact float equality; the whole summary, `differences`, `warnings` and `fidelity_label` included, is equal too), both tables equal row for row, `model.json` byte-equal and equal to the file given, and the manifests differing only in fields of `run_id`, `created`, `started`, `ended`, `host` (here `run_id`, `created`, `started` and `ended`). A third run with `workers=2` is identical to the first. A run at seed 12346 has the same table schemas and row counts but other values, other metrics, another `model.json` and another manifest `seed`, so the comparison is not vacuous.
  - **How tables are compared (rule 5, owner to confirm).** As Arrow tables read from `tables/`, not as file bytes (the council dropped byte-identical Parquet as a property of the writer). pyarrow 26.0.1's `Table.equals` returns `False` for two tables holding NaN in the same cell (tested), and a `replications` table holds NaN for a mean over no items (AKS-98), so `tests/runs/run_comparison.py` compares the schemas (names, types, order) and then every row, each float cell by `float.hex`: equal to the last bit, NaN equal to NaN in the same place, 0.0 apart from −0.0. A second model (customers every 20 h on average, 3 h with 1 h of warm-up, 4 replications) puts NaN in `till.wait.mean`; its two runs are identical by this rule while `Table.equals` says they differ. Spec 1 "Identical runs" now states the rule.
  - **The cross-process test.** `tests/runs/test_cross_process.py` prepares two run folders from the same file (6 replications of 50 h, two scenarios) and starts `python -m lcsim.runs.worker` twice at the same time as separate operating-system processes, with `--workers 1` and `--workers 2`, from different working directories and with `PYTHONHASHSEED` 1 and 2 (spec 1 forbids the salted `hash()`). Their `replications` tables are equal row for row, their `timeseries` tables too, and both equal the `tables/replications.parquet` of a run made through `LocalTarget.execute`. Spec 1 "Identical runs" now says the results do not depend on `workers` or on which process runs a replication (spec 1 "Division of labour" already said so for `workers`).
  - **No product code changed.** Both tests passed on their first run against the AKS-103 build (guide rule 4: the tests were written first; nothing failed, so nothing was implemented). Five further runs of both files passed, as did both Python versions in `bash scripts/check.sh`.
- **Reason:** spec 1 "Identical runs", "Random streams" and the two M3 acceptance rows; guide M3 "Done when" ("a repeat with the same seed is identical as spec 1 defines, also across processes"); the AKS-104 acceptance list and Agent notes.
- **Alternatives considered:**
  - `Table.equals` alone: wrong for NaN cells, which spec 1 requires to be written.
  - Replacing NaN with a sentinel before `Table.equals`: a sentinel could collide with a real value.
  - Comparing the Parquet bytes: dropped by the council; the writer may change its bytes between versions without the rows changing.
  - Comparing floats with `==`: treats 0.0 and −0.0 as equal, which "full precision" does not need to allow.
  - The cross-process row through two `LocalTarget.execute` calls only: each is a separate spawned process, but both are children of one interpreter with one hash seed; the standalone worker shows the result with nothing shared.
- **Spec sections updated:** spec 1 "Identical runs" (how tables are compared; results independent of `workers` and of the process), the acceptance table's Evidence for the cross-process row, and a dated line under "Changes in this version".
- **Dependencies added:** none.

### Performance measured at M3 (AKS-104)

- **Decision:**
  - Spec 2's row "A 30-replication run of the three-machine line finishes in under one minute" is **measured**, not confirmed. `tests/perf/test_three_machine_line.py` (marker `perf`, already registered in `pyproject.toml` and excluded from `check.sh` by `addopts`) runs `examples/three_machine_line.json` unchanged (30 replications of 100 h after 20 h of warm-up, seed 12345) through `LocalTarget.create` and `execute`, and records the measurements, which `tests/perf/conftest.py` prints at the end of the session. With `source .venv/bin/activate && taskset -c 0-3 pytest -m perf` it took 1.52, 1.64 and 1.55 s on Python 3.13.18 and 1.68, 1.79 and 1.58 s on 3.11.18, with 4 workers; 2.65 s under `taskset -c 0` with 1 worker. Peak resident memory: about 219 MiB for the driving pytest process (`RUSAGE_SELF`), 151 to 154 MiB for the largest finished child, the run's worker (`RUSAGE_CHILDREN`), and 549 to 560 MiB for the whole process tree at once (sampled from `/proc` every 0.1 s). Machine: a cloud container with 4 CPUs (Intel Xeon at 2.10 GHz), `os.cpu_count()` 4, affinity set 4 under the protocol, 15.71 GiB of memory. Details and library versions are in `docs/research/perf-M3.md`.
  - **Not the low tier.** This container is not the owner's machine and is not capped to 8 GB; the guide calls a target "confirmed" only when the owner runs the same command on a machine capped to 4 cores and 8 GB (AKS-82). The margin (about 58 s) and the memory (0.56 GiB of 8 GB) make a miss there unlikely, but that is for the owner to record.
  - **`workers` for the measurement (rule 5, owner to confirm).** Spec 3 gives a run "`workers` = cores of the active tier", and tiers do not exist before M6. The check uses the cores the process may use (`os.sched_getaffinity`, else `os.cpu_count()`), which is 4 under the protocol's `taskset -c 0-3`, the low tier's core count.
  - **What is timed.** From `create` to the end of `execute` (loading, validate, the run folder, translate, the spawned worker, collect, the statistics and the Parquet files), since a user waits for all of it.
  - **The known-gaps row** "Performance targets are proposals" says "Measured at M3, M6, M9; confirmed by the owner before M11". The M3 measurement is recorded here and in the session summary; the guide's table is the owner's text and is not edited, as at M1 and M2.
  - `tests/perf/` is a package (`__init__.py`), like `tests/benchmarks/`, so that its `conftest.py` does not clash with the bare `conftest` module of `tests/engines/process` under mypy.
  - **Review of PR #25 (Codex), one finding, confirmed and fixed with a failing test first:** the perf module imported `resource` at the top, which does not exist on Windows, and pytest imports every test module before it deselects `perf`, so the whole suite would fail to collect there. `max_rss_bytes` now imports `resource` when called and returns `None` without it (the record then shows `null`); the core count falls back to `os.cpu_count()` and the tree sample to `None` already. Tests: `tests/perf/test_portability.py` (not marked `perf`, so it runs in `check.sh`) imports the module in a subprocess with `resource` blocked, and reads both peak RSS figures where it exists.
  - **Second review (on 4f0b54f), one finding, confirmed and fixed:** the new RSS test asserted a reading, which is `None` on Windows, so `check.sh` would still fail there. Reproduced by running `tests/perf/test_portability.py` under pytest with `sys.modules["resource"] = None`. A test that runs the folder's other tests that way was written first and failed; the RSS test now expects a reading where `resource` exists and `None` where it does not, with nothing skipped, and a further test checks the `None` fallback in process. Fixed in 9325f29.
- **Reason:** guide "Performance measurement protocol" and M3 "Done when"; spec 2 acceptance table; spec 3 "Run queue" and "Hardware tiers".
- **Alternatives considered:**
  - Timing `execute` alone: leaves out loading and translate, which the user also waits for.
  - A fixed `workers=4`: on a machine with fewer cores the run would oversubscribe them, and the protocol already fixes the cores with `taskset`.
  - Printing with `-s`: the protocol's command has no `-s`, so the measurements are printed by a terminal-summary hook instead.
- **Spec sections updated:** spec 2 acceptance table (Evidence of the perf row) and a dated line under "Changes in this version".
- **Dependencies added:** none.

It was not (owner decision 1, AKS-74, still open): `ls reference/prototype` reports no such directory and `git ls-files reference` lists nothing on this branch. The deletion is a no-op, recorded here.

The owner reviewed the design step by step in a Claude Code session, following a walkthrough page with the wireframes (`docs/design/preview/owner-review.html`). This entry records each answer. Items the owner confirmed as proposed are marked "confirmed"; items the owner changed say how. Quotes are the owner's words.

### Overall direction

- **Decision:** Version 2 of the design system replaces version 1's look. The owner said the first draft "looks very functional" but "does not evoke a wow factor and screams more like a traditional ERP interface", asked for Sanzo Wada's *A Dictionary of Color Combinations* as the colour reference, icons on the navigation and canvas buttons, research on design that keeps users coming back, and charts for data, outputs and searches of many trials ("if lets say 1000 simulations trials are taking to optimize a process, how do I view the same").

- **Spec sections updated:** Spec 3 warning line, "Screens" item 2, "Canvas behaviour" (Delete, External edits, References), "Design before building", "Changes in this version".

Recorded for M9, to be pinned and re-checked from each package's licence file when added: Phosphor Icons `@phosphor-icons/react` 2.1.10 (MIT); fonts Bricolage Grotesque, Atkinson Hyperlegible Next, JetBrains Mono (SIL OFL 1.1, bundled in the app); Apache ECharts 6.1.0 (Apache-2.0) with `zrender` 6.1.0 (BSD-3-Clause) and `echarts-for-react` 3.0.6 (MIT), whose `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` ship in the installer. Colour values are copied from `mattdesl/dictionary-of-colour-combinations` 1.0.2 (MIT), credited in `docs/design/design-system.md`; the dataset itself is not added.
