## 2026-11-07 · M5

### M5 research notes: solver layer, simulation-based optimization, benchmark sources (AKS-132)

- **Decision:**
  - **Solver layer** (`docs/research/spec-2-solver-layer.md`). "HiGHS through Pyomo" and Optuna are confirmed (spec 2 "Optimization"); OR-Tools is not adopted (no Phase 1 problem needs it, and it brings absl-py, pandas and protobuf). The HiGHS adapter (AKS-108) uses Pyomo's `pyomo.contrib.solver` interface, `from pyomo.contrib.solver.common.factory import SolverFactory; SolverFactory("highs")`, which runs HiGHS in-process through `highspy` (no executable, no subprocess), not APPSI (`appsi_highs`) and not the legacy `pyomo.environ.SolverFactory`. It calls `solve(model, load_solutions=False, raise_exception_on_nonoptimal_result=False, tee=False)` and maps `TerminationCondition`: `convergenceCriteriaSatisfied` → `optimal` (then `solution_loader.load_vars()`, `incumbent_objective`, `uslack()`/`lslack()`/`slack()` per constraint, and `get_duals()` for continuous problems only, since an integer program raises `NoDualsError`); `provenInfeasible` or `locallyInfeasible` → `infeasible`; `unbounded` → `unbounded`; `infeasibleOrUnbounded` → one more solve with `solver_options={"presolve": "off"}`, mapped by the same table; anything else → E190 with the condition's name (E192 for `maxTimeLimit` if a time limit is set). "Tight" needs a tolerance (the product mix's binding wood constraint has slack 5.7e-14); 1e-6 is proposed, at least as loose as HiGHS 1.15.1's `primal_feasibility_tolerance` (1e-07) and `mip_feasibility_tolerance` (1e-06). Pyomo calls `pyomo.contrib.solver` a development preview, so AKS-108 keeps every call to it in one module. `Capabilities.licence` of the optimization adapter is `MIT` (HiGHS, the engine), `invocation` `in-process`.
  - **Declared lower bounds**, for AKS-108 and AKS-109 to add: `highspy>=1.15.1`, `pyomo>=6.10.1`, `optuna>=5.0.0`, the newest releases, all installing on Python 3.11 and 3.13 (the AKS-96 to AKS-98 house rule). The lowest versions seen to work on both Pythons are pyomo 6.9.2 (the first tested with `pyomo.contrib.solver.common.factory`; 6.8.0 to 6.9.1 fail to import it) with highspy 1.8.1 (the first with a CPython 3.13 wheel); Optuna 5.0.0 is the first with `Trial.set_constraint`.
  - **Simulation-based optimization** (`docs/research/spec-2-simulation-based-optimization.md`). No ranking-and-selection procedure replaces spec 2's fixed top-three confirmation in Phase 1; it is the clean-up step of Boesel, Nelson and Kim (2003), within Hong and Nelson's (2009) "top 5 to 10%" at the default budget, with no new user setting. KN with common random numbers, with Batur and Kim's feasibility check for the constraints, is recorded as the Phase 2 candidate. Optuna's TPE sampler stays; `GPSampler` is not used (it needs PyTorch).
  - **Seeding recipe for AKS-109:** `optuna.logging.set_verbosity(optuna.logging.WARNING)`; `tpe_seed = int(optimizer_generator(seed).integers(0, 2**32))` (the first and only draw from the key `<seed>:0:optimizer`, since `TPESampler` seeds a `numpy.random.RandomState`, which accepts only 0 to 2³² − 1); `TPESampler(seed=tpe_seed)` with every other argument at its default; an in-memory study with the objective's direction; the baseline enqueued as trial 0; ask and tell one trial at a time (never `n_jobs`, which reseeds the sampler from the operating system); `suggest_int(<id>, <min>, <max>)` per variable in `search.variables` order; `Trial.set_constraint(<id>, value − mean)` for `>=` and `mean − value` for `<=`, `math.inf` for a NaN mean; `tell(trial, mean)`, or `state=TrialState.FAIL` for a NaN objective. A seeded study repeated exactly across runs, processes (`PYTHONHASHSEED` varied) and Python 3.11/3.13, but not across Optuna 4.x, 4.9.0 and 5.0.0, so a repeat holds on the same Optuna and numpy versions, as spec 1 "Identical runs" already requires; AKS-109 records the Optuna version with the run.
  - **Benchmark sources, M5** (`docs/research/spec-2-benchmark-sources.md`, M5 part). Every M5 answer in spec 2 "Benchmark definitions" is re-derived without a solver and stands: product mix 2,200 at (24, 14), unique, duals 1 and 4; knapsack 21 (b = c = 1; 10 of 16 subsets fit); assignment 9 (of 6 permutations); diet 98/45 = 2.178 at (16/9, 10/9); machine count, by Erlang C, 4.500 min at 1 till, 0.245 min (27/110) at 2 tills with utilization 0.375, and 3 to 5 tills below utilization 0.35, so the best is `tills` = 2.
- **Reason:** guide rules 3, 5 and 7; spec 2 "Research before building" (M5: simulation-based optimization, solver layer, benchmark sources); the spec 1 engine licence rule. Versions and licences were read on 2026-11-07 from PyPI JSON and from the licence files inside the downloaded wheels; the interface, status mapping and seeding recipe were run in scratch venvs on Python 3.11.18 and 3.13.18 (each note's "Tested").
- **Alternatives considered:** APPSI (`appsi_highs`), the older Pyomo interface; driving `highspy` directly without Pyomo (spec 2 names Pyomo, and Pyomo's interface already maps HiGHS's statuses, duals and slacks); OR-Tools; KN, Rinott, NSGS or OCBA in place of the top-three confirmation; the sampler argument `constraints_func` (deprecated in Optuna 5.0.0, removal scheduled for 7.0.0) instead of `Trial.set_constraint`; counting a repeated suggestion against the budget; passing the SHA-256 entropy directly to `TPESampler` (rejected by `RandomState`); lower bounds at the oldest working versions (wider, but untested by CI).
- **Awaiting owner** (rule 5, simplest readings for AKS-108 and AKS-109 to build and write into spec 2, owner to confirm): (1) a termination other than optimal, infeasible or unbounded is E190 (E192 for a time limit), since spec 2 has no fourth `status`; (2) "tight" means |slack| ≤ 1e-6; (3) `Capabilities.licence` names HiGHS's `MIT` only, Pyomo's BSD-3-Clause being recorded here; (4) a repeated Optuna suggestion is not re-simulated, does not count against `budget`, and asking stops after 10 × `budget` asks; (5) lower bounds at the newest releases rather than the oldest working ones; (6) the confirmation stage, as spec 2 is written, re-runs replications from 0 with the experiment seed, so its first `search.replications` values are the search stage's own (streams are keyed `<seed>:<replication>:<component id>` and the adapter numbers replications from 0) and the confirmed means keep part of the search's selection bias; AKS-109 builds it as written, and the owner decides whether confirmation replications are numbered from `search.replications` instead, disjoint from the search's while keeping common random numbers across confirmed candidates (found in review of PR #30).
- **Licences checked** (spec 1 engine licence rule, AKS-83 open; not legal advice): highspy 1.15.1 MIT (bundling AMD BSD-3, CLI11 BSD-3, METIS and GKlib Apache-2.0, pdqsort zlib, RCM MIT, zstr MIT, from `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`; the wheel is built with HiPO, so the METIS, AMD and RCM code is in it); pyomo 6.10.1 BSD-3-Clause (with BSD-style notices for FOQUS, IDAES, PLY, PyUtilib, WST and WaterTAP) and no runtime dependencies; optuna 5.0.0 MIT (with SciPy BSD-3 and fdlibm code) and its dependencies alembic 1.20.1 MIT, SQLAlchemy 2.1.8 MIT, Mako 1.4.3 MIT, MarkupSafe 3.0.4 BSD-3-Clause, typing_extensions 4.16.0 PSF-2.0, colorlog 6.12.0 MIT, packaging 26.3 Apache-2.0 OR BSD-2-Clause, tqdm 4.70.1 MPL-2.0 AND MIT (no "Incompatible With Secondary Licenses" notice, so MPL-2.0 section 3.3 allows the AGPL-3.0 combination), PyYAML 6.0.3 MIT; numpy (already a dependency); ortools 9.15.6755 Apache-2.0 (not adopted, dependencies not checked). All compatible with AGPL-3.0, so HiGHS, Pyomo and Optuna may be imported in-process, as spec 1 says.
- **Spec sections updated:** none. No finding contradicts spec 1 or spec 2: the confirmation stage stays, the benchmark answers stand, and a seeded search repeats on the same library versions, which spec 1 "Identical runs" already makes the condition. Spec 2 "Changes in this version" gets no "Since version 0.7" line from this ticket.
- **Dependencies added:** none. `pyproject.toml` is unchanged; AKS-108 adds `highspy` and `pyomo`, AKS-109 adds `optuna`, each with the bounds and licences recorded above.

### HiGHS adapter through Pyomo; the optimization stand-in replaced; E175 (AKS-108)

- **Decision:**
  - **The adapter.** `lcsim.engines.optimize.adapter:HighsAdapter`, entry point `highs` in group `lcsim.engines`, version the product version. `capabilities()`: `domains` `optimization`, no component types, no hardware, no parallel modes (one solve per run), `licence` `MIT` (HiGHS, the engine; Pyomo BSD-3-Clause recorded here), `invocation` `in-process`. `translate` writes `engine/input.json` (the program with every number a float); `run` solves once and writes `engine/output/solution.json` (the spec 2 layout, the duals of an all-continuous program, and HiGHS's name and version); `collect` returns the layout as `Results.solution` with no tables. Every Pyomo call is in `lcsim.engines.optimize.solver`, as the research note asked, since Pyomo calls `pyomo.contrib.solver` a development preview.
  - **The research note's claims, each verified by a test** in `tests/engines/optimize/test_solver.py` against the installed Pyomo 6.10.1 and HiGHS 1.15.1: the product mix is `convergenceCriteriaSatisfied` at (24, 14) with objective 2,200 and duals 1 and 4; the diet's duals are 11/45 and 1/45; an integer program's `get_duals()` raises `NoDualsError`; an infeasible LP and integer program are `provenInfeasible`; an unbounded LP is `unbounded`; the unbounded integer program is `infeasibleOrUnbounded` with default options and `unbounded` on the second solve with `presolve` `off`; `simplex_iteration_limit` 0 gives `iterationLimit` and `time_limit` 0 gives `maxTimeLimit`, both stopping the solve (E190). One finding the note did not have: a variable that no objective term or constraint names is left with value `None` by Pyomo, so every variable enters the objective (coefficient 0 where the objective does not name it), and HiGHS then gives it a value within its bounds. Two more: Pyomo cannot build a constraint with no terms (`InvalidConstraintError`), and a model with no variables ends `unknown`; both are judged without the solver (below).
  - **The stand-in is gone.** `STAND_IN_ENGINES` is removed from `lcsim.model.validation`; `InstalledEngine.name` is always an adapter name; `installed_engines` lists the adapters only; `select_adapter` returns an adapter whenever it reports no issue. An `optimization` model is checked against the discovered adapters, so `run.engine` naming no installed optimization adapter is E102 at `/run/engine`, and with no optimization adapter installed the model is E102 at `/domain`.
  - **E175** (M1 leftover, AKS-88 and AKS-89): a coefficient in the objective or a constraint whose key names no variable in `variables` is a new meaning-pass code E175, at the coefficient's key (`/objective/linear/<name>`, `/constraints/<i>/linear/<name>`), `component` `null`, with a hint listing the variables (up to 12). A new code rather than E150 (a parameter reference) or E174 (a metric name), since a variable is neither; numbered after E174, the other "objective or constraint names something that does not exist". Fixture `E175_coefficient_names_no_variable.json`.
- **Reason:** guide rules 5, 6 and 7 (HiGHS is wrapped, nothing is solved here); spec 1 "Engine contract", "Top-level keys" and "Validation issues"; spec 2 "Optimization" and "Mathematical optimization in the model file"; `docs/research/spec-2-solver-layer.md` (AKS-132).
- **Awaiting owner** (rule 5, simplest readings; each written into spec 1 or spec 2 and marked owner to confirm):
  1. The adapter is named `highs`, after its engine, as `simpy` is. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  2. E175 is a new code for a coefficient that names no variable (above). Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  3. A termination other than optimal, infeasible or unbounded (and `infeasibleOrUnbounded` after the second solve) fails the run with E190, the condition named in `log.txt` through the exception text, since an issue message never holds exception text; no HiGHS time limit is set, so the run target's time limit (E192) applies and `maxTimeLimit` never arises from a product run. Research note decision 1. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  4. Tight means |slack| ≤ 1e-6. Research note decision 2. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  5. Values, the objective and slacks are reported as HiGHS gives them, unrounded (24.000000000000004 chairs, a wood slack of 5.7e-14); the interface rounds for display. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  6. For `infeasible` and `unbounded`, `objective` is `null` and `variables` and `constraints` are empty objects. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  7. A constraint with an empty `linear` is judged exactly without the solver (left-hand side 0), and a violated one makes the model `infeasible`; a model with no variables is `optimal` at objective 0 when every constraint holds. Spec 2 does not forbid either. Candidate bucket: drop? (the owner may prefer an error code for both).
  8. Duals are kept in `engine/output/solution.json` only, not in `summary.json`, whose spec 2 layout has none. Candidate bucket: Phase 2 (sensitivity reporting).
  9. `Capabilities.licence` is HiGHS's `MIT` only (research note decision 3). Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  10. `translate`'s `experiment_id` is `None` for an optimization model (spec 1 "Engine adapter interface" typed it `str`); a process adapter given `None`, or the optimization adapter given an id, raises `ValueError`. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  11. An optimization run is started without an experiment id (`LocalTarget.create(model_file)`, `run_experiment(adapter, model, None, workdir)`), as spec 3 has `POST .../runs` take none; an id given for it, or none for a process model, raises `ValueError` before anything is created, as an unknown experiment id already did (the service maps it to 400 at M6). Its manifest has `experiment_id` `null`, `seed` `null` (nothing is sampled; HiGHS's own `random_seed` stays at its default) and `scenario_ids` `[]`, and the run folder has no `tables/`, since there are no tables. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  12. The optimization benchmark files: rows `objective` and `variables.<id>`, `sig_figs_4`, exact values, the variables checked as well as the objective (spec 2 asks only for the objective; the ticket names the variables too), no experiment or seed, skipped by the coverage run, and a non-optimal status fails every row. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
- **Engine gap: Pyomo's HiGHS interface lacks interrupting a running solve.** `opt.solve` returns only when HiGHS finishes; `pyomo.contrib.solver.solvers.highs` 6.10.1 offers no hook for a cancel token, only HiGHS's keyboard-interrupt handling (`HandleKeyboardInterrupt = True` before `run()`, read in the installed source), which a `multiprocessing.Event` cannot reach. Workaround: `run` checks `cancel` before and after the solve and writes nothing when it is set; a longer solve is ended by the local target, which terminates the worker 5 s after the cancel (spec 1 "How the `local` target runs a run"), and by the time limit (E192). Spec sections: spec 1 "Engine adapter interface" (`run` checks `cancel` at least once a second) and "Division of labour".
- **Benchmarks.** `benchmarks/product_mix`, `knapsack`, `assignment` and `diet` pass under `pytest -m benchmark` at `CURRENT_MILESTONE` M5: HiGHS 1.15.1 gives product mix 2200.0 at chairs 24.000000000000004 and tables 13.999999999999998 (both constraints tight, wood slack 5.7e-14, duals 0.9999999999999996 and 4.0), knapsack 21.0 with b = c = 1 and a = d = 0, assignment 9.0 with x_0_1 = x_1_0 = x_2_2 = 1, diet 2.177777777777778 at x 1.777777777777778 and y 1.1111111111111112 (duals 11/45 and 1/45). The M2 and M4 benchmarks still pass. The machine-count benchmark is AKS-109's.
- **Left:** a whole number too large for a float in a coefficient, bound or `rhs` loads (the loader accepts any JSON integer) and fails the run with E190 at `translate` (`OverflowError`), with no validation code; numbers of magnitude 1e20 or more are HiGHS's infinity (a `<=` with `rhs` 1e30 is no constraint). Neither has a spec 1 code; left for the owner, candidate bucket Phase 2.
- **Spec sections updated:** spec 1 "Validation" (E175 among the meaning-pass codes), "Engine contract" (the `highs` adapter), "Top-level keys" (no stand-in), "Validation issues" (E175), "Engine adapter interface" (`translate`'s `experiment_id`, the optimization adapter's `Capabilities`), "Division of labour" (the optimization adapter's files and cancel); spec 2 warning line and "Mathematical optimization in the model file" (how the adapter fills `summary.json`). Spec 1 "How the `local` target runs a run" (started without an experiment id; the manifest's `null` fields; no `tables/`). Spec 2 "Benchmark definitions" (how the M5 optimization files read the table) and its acceptance row's evidence. All under "Since version 0.7".
- **Dependencies added:**
  - `highspy>=1.15.1` (HiGHS 1.15.1), MIT, from the installed wheel's `highspy-1.15.1.dist-info/licenses/LICENSE.txt` ("MIT License, Copyright (c) 2026 HiGHS"), with `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` listing amd, cli, metis, pdqsort, rcm and zstr, whose licences the AKS-132 note read (BSD-3, BSD-3, Apache-2.0, zlib, MIT, MIT). Runtime dependency: numpy (already a dependency, AKS-96). Compatible with AGPL-3.0 under the spec 1 engine licence rule (AKS-83 open; not legal advice).
  - `pyomo>=6.10.1`, BSD-3-Clause, from the installed wheel's `pyomo-6.10.1.dist-info/licenses/LICENSE.md` (3-clause BSD, Sandia, with "Additional Notices" for Foqus, IDAES, PLY, PyUtilib, WST and WaterTAP, as the AKS-132 note read them); `Requires-Dist` names no runtime dependency (only extras). Compatible with AGPL-3.0 as above.
  - How checked: both installed with `pip install -e ".[dev]"` on Python 3.13.18 in the project venv, and `highspy==1.15.1 pyomo==6.10.1` in a fresh Python 3.11.18 venv in the scratch folder (numpy 2.4.6 came with them; no other distribution); the probe of the status cases ran there with the same output as on 3.13. Licence metadata read with `importlib.metadata` in both venvs: `License-Expression` `MIT` and `BSD-3-Clause`. mypy: pyomo ships no `py.typed`, so `pyproject.toml` gives `pyomo.*` `ignore_missing_imports` (highspy has `py.typed` and is not imported directly).

### Simulation-based search with Optuna; the machine-count test and benchmark (AKS-109)

- **Decision:**
  - **The search** is `lcsim.runs.search`, a driver inside the experiment manager (spec 2: "not an adapter"). It never runs a model itself: each candidate evaluation is one `translate`, `run` and `collect` of the model's own adapter in its own subfolder of `engine/` (spec 1 "Division of labour"), in-process for `run_experiment` and through one spawned worker per evaluation on the local target. `check_experiment` and `LocalTarget.create` no longer raise `NotImplementedError` for a search (M3 "Left"); a search run has the states, `run_id`, manifest and single time limit of any run.
  - **No ranking-and-selection procedure replaces the fixed top-three confirmation** (AKS-132 research note, decision 1): spec 2's confirmation is built as written, the baseline always confirmed, then groups of three feasible-at-search candidates until one is confirmed feasible or none remain.
  - **The research note's recipe, each step tested** in `tests/runs/test_search.py`: logging at `WARNING`; `tpe_seed = int(optimizer_generator(seed).integers(0, 2**32))` (3441887045 for seed 12345, as the note found); `TPESampler(seed=tpe_seed)` with its other defaults; an in-memory study in the objective's direction; the baseline enqueued as trial 0; ask and tell one trial at a time; `suggest_int` per `integer` variable; `Trial.set_constraint` per constraint (`math.inf` for an undefined mean); `TrialState.FAIL` for an undefined objective; a repeated suggestion reuses the stored results and does not count against the budget, and asking stops at 10 × `budget` asks (tested with a search that can only reach two candidates: 30 asks for budget 3). The machine-count Optuna path proposes 1, 1, 2, 4 for seed 12345, the note's sequence, so its candidates are 1, 2 and 4 after 4 asks. No warning was raised in any run (`-W error` on the recipe with constraints, failed trials and `suggest_float`).
  - **Machine count.** `tests/runs/test_search.py` (marker `benchmark`): with budget 10 (exhaustive) the search finds `tills` = 2, equal to the enumerated best of all five values run as scenarios at the experiment's 30 replications, and each confirmed candidate's metrics equal the enumeration's (same seed, common random numbers); with budget 3 (Optuna) the best is 2, feasible and confirmed, and a second run gives the same summary. `benchmarks/machine_count` holds the exhaustive case with rows from Erlang C; at seed 12345: 1 till 267.63 s (−0.50 SE from 270), 2 tills 14.572 s (−0.67 SE from 162/11) at utilization 0.37431 (−0.61 SE from 0.375); 3 to 5 tills infeasible at the search stage. Its coverage run passed 20 of 20 seeds from 12345.
  - **The baseline-only-feasible case** (the owner's comment on AKS-109, from the PR #1 review) is `test_the_baseline_alone_feasible_on_confirmation_is_the_best`: the baseline fails the constraint at the search stage (utilization mean 0 over 10 replications) and passes confirmed (2/3 over 30), every other candidate passes at search and fails confirmed (1/3); all five are confirmed, the baseline is the best and `after` equals `before`.
  - **Review of PR #32 (Codex):** the path was chosen by listing every integer combination before comparing their number with the budget, so a range of 10⁹ values could fill memory when the run was created (the manifest's `optimizer`) or when the search started, even with budget 3. `combination_count` now multiplies the range lengths, `search_method` and the progress plan use it, and the combinations are listed only on the exhaustive path, once they are known to fit the budget (`test_a_large_integer_range_is_counted_not_enumerated`, with two variables of 10⁹ values each and `itertools.product` made to fail). Spec 2 already said "the product of their ranges"; no spec change.
  - **Second review round of PR #32 (Codex):** every candidate kept the tables its `collect` returned, so the driving process's memory grew with the budget, outside the worker's memory limit (E192). Now an evaluation keeps its metrics and the objective's value per replication (for `separated`); the search stage drops its tables at once, and of the confirmations only the baseline's and the best so far keep theirs, the summary's and `tables/`'s source (`test_only_the_baseline_and_best_confirmations_keep_their_tables`). The tables stay on disk in each evaluation's folder. No spec change: the results are the same.
  - **The overlap is built as written** (AKS-132 Awaiting owner 6): the confirmation's replications are numbered from 0, so its first `search.replications` values are the search stage's (`test_confirmation_repeats_the_search_stage_replications_as_spec_2_is_written`, through the SimPy adapter). The confirmed means dilute the selection bias and do not remove it; spec 2 and the tests say so. The disjoint numbering is not built.
- **Reason:** guide rules 3, 5, 6 and 7 (Optuna proposes, the product enumerates a small integer grid and judges closed-form means; nothing is solved here); spec 1 "Experiments", "Random streams", "Division of labour"; spec 2 "Simulation-based optimization" and its machine-count benchmark and acceptance row; `docs/research/spec-2-simulation-based-optimization.md`.
- **Awaiting owner** (rule 5, simplest readings; each written into spec 1 or spec 2 and marked owner to confirm):
  1. A parameter named twice in `search.variables` is E171 at the repeat (M1 leftover). Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  2. A `search` in an experiment that lists scenarios is E171 at `scenarios`: spec 2's result has one set of metrics per candidate, so a search runs its own candidates, not scenarios (an empty `scenarios` is allowed). Candidate bucket: Phase 1a (searching within each scenario: Phase 2).
  3. A candidate is the experiment with one `default` scenario holding its values and the stage's replication count, everything else unchanged; its evaluations are in `engine/search-<n>` and `engine/confirm-<n>`, n from `000`, the baseline. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  4. The exhaustive path needs every variable to be `integer` ("distinct integer combinations"); it runs the baseline, then the other combinations with the first variable changing slowest. A `number` or `quantity` variable is suggested by Optuna with `suggest_float` (a quantity in seconds), so such a search always takes the Optuna path. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  5. The manifest of a search run has `optimizer` `{name, version}`: `optuna` with the Optuna version, or `exhaustive` with `null` (research note decision 8 left the place to AKS-109). Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  6. Feasible means a defined objective mean and every constraint holding on defined means; a constraint on an undefined mean does not hold, and an undefined objective is never feasible (told to Optuna as a failed trial). Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  7. Equal means rank by candidate order, the earlier first; the confirmation groups exclude the baseline, which is confirmed first. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  8. `separated` compares the best with the next confirmed feasible candidate by the ranking, paired by replication number at the experiment's `confidence`; `false` when there is none, and when `status` is `infeasible`. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  9. `search` gains `status`, `feasible` or `infeasible`: spec 2 names `status` `infeasible` but its layout had no place for it. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  10. A candidate's `metrics` and `feasible` in `candidates` are its confirmation's when confirmed, otherwise its search stage's (`n` shows which); `before` and `after` are `null` when undefined. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  11. The rest of a search run's `summary.json` and its `tables/` are the best candidate's confirmation: `replications` the experiment's, `scenarios` the one `default` with the best's `set`, `differences` empty; the warnings after the load and `validate` warnings are that confirmation's `collect` warnings, and the other evaluations' `collect` warnings are not kept. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  12. The warm-up check (W210) of a search checks only the best candidate's confirmation, scenario `default` (spec 2 left the scope to M5). Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  13. Progress weights each evaluation by its replications against the planned total (budget or the combination count at the search stage, the baseline and one group at confirmation), never falls, and stays below 1 until `done`; a cancel stops the running evaluation and starts no other; one time limit for the whole search. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  14. The machine-count benchmark file: rows `search.best.tills` (`sig_figs_4`, one value) and `search.candidates[tills=<v>].<metric>` (`three_se` over a confirmation's replications); the file keeps the coffee shop's experiment (30 replications, seed 12345) and `search.replications` 10; the coverage run includes it; the Optuna path is checked by the test, not by a file. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
- **Spec sections updated:** spec 1 warning line, "Experiments" (E171's new cases; "Proposed; verified at M5" replaced), "Random streams" (the one optimizer draw), "Run folder and results" (`optimizer` in the manifest; a search run's summary and tables), "Division of labour" (the evaluation subfolders), "How the `local` target runs a run" (a search), "Validation issues" (E171 row); spec 2 warning line, "Simulation-based optimization" ("Proposed; verified at M5" replaced by the readings above), "Statistic definitions" (the warm-up check's scope), "Benchmark definitions" (the machine-count file) and the acceptance row's evidence. All under "Since version 0.7".
- **Dependencies added:**
  - `optuna>=5.0.0` (5.0.0, the first with `Trial.set_constraint`; research note decision on the lower bound), MIT, from the installed wheel's `optuna-5.0.0.dist-info/licenses/LICENSE` ("MIT License, Copyright (c) 2018 Preferred Networks, Inc.") and `LICENSE_THIRD_PARTY` (code from SciPy under the BSD 3-clause licence, and fdlibm, as the AKS-132 note read them). Optuna ships `py.typed`, so mypy needs no override.
  - Its runtime dependencies, as the AKS-132 note listed them, confirmed from each installed distribution's licence file on 2026-11-07: alembic 1.20.1 MIT (`licenses/LICENSE`, "Copyright 2009-2026 Michael Bayer"), SQLAlchemy 2.1.8 MIT, Mako 1.4.3 MIT, MarkupSafe 3.0.4 BSD-3-Clause ("Copyright 2010 Pallets"), typing_extensions 4.16.0 PSF-2.0 (the Python licence text), colorlog 6.12.0 MIT, packaging 26.3 Apache-2.0 OR BSD-2-Clause (`LICENSE`, `LICENSE.APACHE`, `LICENSE.BSD`), tqdm 4.70.1 MPL-2.0 AND MIT (`LICENCE`: MPL-2.0 for most files with Exhibit A only, no Exhibit B "Incompatible With Secondary Licenses", so MPL-2.0 section 3.3 allows the combination with AGPL-3.0; MIT for the rest), PyYAML 6.0.3 MIT, and numpy (already a dependency). SQLAlchemy pulled in no `greenlet` on either Python. Every licence is compatible with AGPL-3.0 under the spec 1 engine licence rule, as the AKS-132 entry found (AKS-83 open; not legal advice).
  - How checked: `pip install -e ".[dev]"` on Python 3.13.18 in the project venv and on 3.11.18 in a fresh venv in the scratch folder (numpy 2.4.6, scipy 1.17.1, pint 0.25.3 there); a script walked `importlib.metadata` requirements from `optuna`, evaluating markers with no extras, and printed each distribution's licence metadata and the head of every licence file; both Pythons gave the same tree and versions apart from numpy.

### M5 closed (AKS-109)

- **Decision:**
  - M5 is closed. Every gate in the guide's M5 "Done when" row and every spec acceptance row tagged M5 passes on the top of the M5 stack (PRs #30 and #31 plus this change). Evidence is in `docs/sessions/2026-11-07-M5.md`:
    - product mix returns 24, 14 and 2,200 (HiGHS 1.15.1: 2200.0 at 24.000000000000004 and 13.999999999999998), and knapsack (21), assignment (9) and diet (2.177777777777778) pass, all by `sig_figs_4` (spec 2 acceptance "Mathematical optimization benchmarks pass");
    - the machine-count test finds `tills` = 2 and equals the enumerated best (spec 2 acceptance row, M5): exhaustive with budget 10, Optuna with budget 3, the second repeating exactly; `benchmarks/machine_count` passes, and its coverage run passed 20 of 20 seeds;

The known-gaps rows assigned to M5 have their outcomes: "The optimization layout in the model file has never run" is closed for the four M5 programs, the product mix run first (AKS-108), and the indexed layout waits for the transport template at M10, as the row says; "Engine licences were never checked against AGPL-3.0" is recorded for HiGHS, Pyomo and Optuna with every runtime dependency (AKS-132, AKS-108, AKS-109), AKS-83 still open. As at M1 to M4, the guide's table is the owner's verbatim text and is not edited.

## 2026-11-07 · Owner decisions on M5 "Awaiting owner" items

The owner answered four M5 items in the orchestrating Claude Code session on 2026-11-07. Their words are quoted, then the reading taken. The item numbers are those of `docs/sessions/2026-11-07-M5.md`, "Awaiting owner".

### Item 6: confirmation runs use fresh random numbers (Phase 1a, AKS-133)

- **Owner:** "confirmation runs use fresh random numbers - If we implement an audit module or logs, we need to trace it back. Incl a Phase1a."
- **Decision:**
  - Confirmation replications are numbered from `search.replications` onward. They draw random numbers the search stage never used, and keep common random numbers across the confirmed candidates.
  - Each evaluation records its stage, candidate, seed and replication range, so every confirmed mean can be traced back to its random streams.
  - Bucket: Phase 1a. Linear AKS-133.
  - Until then, M5 runs as built (spec 2 as written).
- **Reason:** removes the selection bias that reusing the search stage's numbers leaves (AKS-132 note; AKS-109 entry). The owner wants it auditable.
- **Spec sections updated:** none yet. AKS-133 changes spec 2 "Simulation-based optimization" and spec 1 "Random streams" and "Run folder and results" first.

### Item 7: keep the top-three confirmation, add ranking and selection, and choose between them (bucket set at the Phase 1 review)

- **Owner:** "No, Can we do both as well as voting process via a model? or score which will decide the method?"
- **Decision:**
  - Ranking and selection does not replace the top-three confirmation. Both are kept: the fixed top-three confirmation, and a ranking-and-selection procedure (KN with common random numbers, plus Batur and Kim's feasibility check, per the AKS-132 note).
  - Which to use, or how to combine their answers, is decided by a selection step: a transparent score from the search's own facts, or a model that weighs the methods' results ("voting").
  - Recorded as the owner's direction. Its design is not settled here. The Phase 1 review (AKS-130) sets the bucket and specifies the score or the vote; Phase 2 is suggested, since it needs research and a spec 2 design.
- **Reason:** the owner prefers keeping both methods and letting a score or vote choose, over replacing one with the other.
- **Spec sections updated:** none yet.

### Item 14: an optimization model with no variables, and a constraint with an empty `linear`, are rejected (Phase 1a)

- **Owner:** "not sure why is the case. I mean when building an optimiser, we'll need variables via the simulation or manually made by the user."
- **Decision:**
  - These two cases should not be answered at all. Today an optimization model with no variables reports `optimal` at 0, and a constraint with an empty `linear` is judged without the solver (AKS-108). Instead, both are validation errors, with an issue code that says what is missing.
  - Bucket: Phase 1a, collected by AKS-130.
- **Reason:** an optimizer needs variables, whether made by the user or produced by a simulation. A model without them is incomplete, not solvable.
- **Spec sections updated:** none yet. The Phase 1a change adds the code to spec 1 "Validation issues" first, with a fixture.

### Item 5 (AKS-83): the engine licence rule is assessed once the product is built (deferred)

- **Owner:** "License rule we should assess once the product is made. We can push it for later."
- **Decision:**
  - The engine licence rule and the AGPL-3.0-only versus -or-later choice are deferred until the product is built. AKS-83 stays open.
  - Meanwhile, every new dependency is still recorded in DECISIONS with its licence and how it was checked (guide "Conventions"), so that the later assessment has the facts.
- **Reason:** the owner's call.
- **Spec sections updated:** none.

## 2026-11-07 · M6

### Service framework confirmed: FastAPI, served by uvicorn with websockets (AKS-110)

- **Decision:** the research item of spec 3 "Research before building" (M6, service half) is `docs/research/spec-3-service-framework-check.md`. FastAPI is confirmed for the engine service (`fastapi>=0.162.1`), created with `docs_url`, `redoc_url` and `openapi_url` set to `None` (so the route table holds only spec 3's routes) and all its built-in telemetry off (FastAPI 0.162 depends on `opentelemetry-api`; with no SDK it is a no-op, and switching off auto-configuration means no `OTEL_*` variable can make the service send anything). The ASGI server is plain `uvicorn>=0.54.0` with `websockets>=17.4` and `ws="websockets-sansio"`, never `uvicorn[standard]`, whose uvloop has no Windows build; uvicorn's legacy `websockets` protocol is deprecated since 0.50.0. Tests use Starlette's `TestClient` on `httpx2>=2.16.0` (development only), since Starlette 1.10 deprecates `httpx` for its test client; the service's own probe uses `urllib.request` with no proxy. `python-multipart` 0.0.33 (Apache-2.0) is the parser AKS-113 will add for uploads. No `psutil`: hardware is read with the standard library and `ctypes`.
- **Reason:** guide rule 3 (research before code) and "Conventions" (each dependency recorded with its licence); spec 3 "Architecture" (Phase 1 native on Windows, proposed default, AKS-77 open: every package must have a Windows build or be pure Python); spec 3 acceptance "The app works with outbound network blocked".
- **Alternatives considered:** Starlette alone (fewer dependencies, but hand-written body checks, and spec 3 names FastAPI); `uvicorn[standard]` (uvloop, httptools, watchfiles: none needed, uvloop not on Windows); `wsproto` (pure Python, MIT; `websockets` has Windows wheels and is uvicorn's default); `httpx` with `ASGITransport` for tests (no WebSocket support, and deprecated for Starlette's test client); `psutil` for memory (BSD-3-Clause, one more compiled package for two numbers).
- **Spec sections updated:** spec 3 "Architecture" (FastAPI confirmed, uvicorn with websockets named) and the warning line. Dated line under spec 3 "Changes in this version".
- **Dependencies added** (versions installed on 2026-11-07; licences read from each installed distribution's licence file, the tree walked with `importlib.metadata` from the roots with markers evaluated and no extras, on Python 3.13.18 in the project venv and 3.11.18 in a fresh scratch venv, the same tree on both; not legal advice, AKS-83 deferred):
  - `fastapi>=0.162.1` (0.162.1), MIT (`fastapi-0.162.1.dist-info/licenses/LICENSE`, "Copyright (c) 2018 Sebastián Ramírez"). Its runtime dependencies: starlette 1.10.0 BSD-3-Clause ("Copyright © 2018, Encode OSS Ltd"), pydantic 2.14.3 MIT, pydantic-core 2.50.4 MIT (compiled; wheels for Windows on cp311 and cp313), annotated-types 0.8.0 MIT, typing-inspection 0.4.5 MIT, annotated-doc 0.0.5 MIT, opentelemetry-api 1.45.2 Apache-2.0 (the Apache License 2.0 text), anyio 4.16.0 MIT, idna 3.20 BSD-3-Clause, typing_extensions 4.16.0 PSF-2.0 (already present through optuna).
  - `uvicorn>=0.54.0` (0.54.0), BSD-3-Clause ("Copyright © 2017-present, Encode OSS Ltd"); its runtime dependencies click 8.5.1 BSD-3-Clause ("Copyright 2014 Pallets") and h11 0.16.0 MIT.
  - `websockets>=17.4` (17.4), BSD-3-Clause ("Copyright (c) Aymeric Augustin and contributors"); no runtime dependencies; wheels for Windows on cp311 and cp313 and a pure-Python wheel.
  - Development only: `httpx2>=2.16.0` (2.16.0), BSD-3-Clause; its dependencies httpcore2 2.16.0 BSD-3-Clause, truststore 0.10.5 MIT, anyio, idna and h11 as above.
  - Every licence is permissive (MIT, BSD-3-Clause, Apache-2.0, PSF-2.0) and compatible with AGPL-3.0 for a combined work. mypy needs no override: fastapi, starlette, pydantic, uvicorn and httpx2 ship `py.typed`.

### Service skeleton: token, discovery, hardening, lifetime, tiers, read endpoints and projects (AKS-110)

- **Decision:**
  - **Layout.** `src/lcsim/service/`: `app.py` (the factory `create_app(config)`, `ROUTERS` one per area, and `route_table(app)` for AKS-114, which lists the routes inside FastAPI 0.162's included routers through `fastapi.routing.iter_route_contexts`, a WebSocket route as `GET` as spec 3's table writes it), `config.py` (`ServiceConfig`: folders, port, token, allowed origins, idle limit and callback, hardware, adapters, clock; all injectable so tests use temporary folders), `auth.py` (the `Hardening` middleware, `authenticate_websocket`, `request_client`), `errors.py` (`ServiceError`, the error body, handlers for FastAPI's validation errors, Starlette's 404 and 405 and any unexpected exception), `lifetime.py`, `discovery.py`, `hardware.py`, `state.py` (`ServiceState` on `app.state.service`, read with the dependency `service_state`), `projects_store.py` (project folders and `projects.json`), and the routers `system.py`, `engines.py`, `templates.py`, `projects.py`; `main.py` and `__main__.py` for `python -m lcsim.service`. A later ticket adds its router to `ROUTERS`. FastAPI 0.162 keeps a reference to an included router rather than copying its routes, so a test adds a test-only route to its own app instance, never to a product router.
  - **The WebSocket token helper** (`authenticate_websocket`) is built and tested on test-only routes added to a test's own app; the production app has no WebSocket route until AKS-111 and AKS-112, so its route table holds only spec 3 rows.
  - **Endpoints built:** `GET /v1/system`, `GET /v1/engines`, `GET /v1/schema`, `GET /v1/schema/components/{type}`, `GET /v1/templates`, `GET /v1/templates/{tid}`, `GET /v1/projects`, `POST /v1/projects` (with `domain`), `POST /v1/projects/open`. Both default models validate with no issues at all through loading and the adapter's `validate` (`tests/service/test_projects.py`).
  - **Component descriptions.** `BlockType` gains `description`, one sentence summarising each `process` type's Behaviour in spec 2 "Block reference"; the SimPy adapter's `capabilities()` still equals the catalogue, since it returns it.
  - `CURRENT_MILESTONE` is `M6` in `tests/benchmarks/conftest.py`.
  - The token comes from `secrets` (32 bytes, base64url without padding, 43 characters): a secret, not simulation randomness, so the spec 1 stream scheme does not apply.
- **Reason:** guide rules 5, 6 and the M6 row; spec 3 "Service rules", "Hardware tiers", "Endpoints", "Status codes and errors", "Project folder"; the owner design review's "`domain` on project creation (M6)" (2026-11-06); the AKS-110 acceptance list.
- **Alternatives considered:** a route-level token dependency instead of middleware (every future router would have to remember it; the middleware also covers unknown paths, so they do not reveal which paths exist); `422` for malformed bodies (spec 3 keeps 422 for `POST .../runs`); 400 for a refused `Host` as Starlette's `TrustedHostMiddleware` answers (403 matches the refused WebSocket upgrade, which the server answers with 403); environment variables for the folders (the CLI options are enough for scripts and tests); storing `index.sqlite` at creation (it is a cache AKS-112 builds from `runs/`).
- **Awaiting owner** (rule 5, simplest readings; each written into spec 1 or spec 3 and marked owner to confirm):
  1. Six service codes, E182 token, E183 hardening, E184 malformed request, E185 not found (404, and 405), E186 project folder, E187 internal failure, and the statuses 403, 405 and 500 added to spec 3's table; for E184 the issues' `path` points into the request body, as E180's `/edits/<n>` does. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  2. `Host` and WebSocket `Origin` rejection is 403 with E183, checked before the token; an upgrade without `Origin` (scripts, the MCP server) passes; until M9 fixes the interface's origin no `Origin` is allowed. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  3. Every HTTP route needs the token, `GET /v1/system` and unknown paths included; 401 carries `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer`; a WebSocket without the right first message is closed with 1008 and the reason `E182`, and a binary first message counts as wrong. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  4. The configuration and user data folders per platform (`%APPDATA%`/`%LOCALAPPDATA%`, `~/Library/Application Support`, XDG), with `--config-dir` and `--data-dir`. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  5. `service.json` is written before serving through a temporary file and a rename, and removed on exit if it still names this service; the second start's probe sends the file's token, bypasses proxies, waits 2 s, and on a 200 prints the port and exits with status 0. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  6. Idle means no run, no open WebSocket and no HTTP request in progress, counted from the end of the last; a request refused for its token does not count (AKS-78 still open; 30 minutes as proposed). Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  7. Hardware: `cores` are the logical processors the process may use; memory counts as reaching a tier at 90% of its nominal size; unknown memory gives `low`; graphics vendors are read on Linux only and are `null` on Windows and macOS; graphics does not set the tier. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a (graphics detection on Windows and macOS: Phase 2, or drop? while no Phase 1 engine uses graphics).
  8. The tier of the first start is stored as `tier` in `settings.json` in the configuration folder and kept by later starts, even if the hardware changes, until Settings changes it (M9). Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  9. `workers` is the lesser of the active tier's cores (4, 8, 16) and the usable cores, reported by `GET /v1/system`; AKS-112 gives it to runs (M3 used the usable cores). Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  10. A request without `X-Client` counts as `script`; another value is 400 with E184. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  11. `GET /v1/schema` is spec 1's schema plus the top-level annotation `x-component-types` (which changes nothing the schema accepts), and a component type is `{type, domain, engine, description, inputs, outputs, outputs_from, settings, metrics}` with each setting's catalogue keys that differ from their defaults, a time default as `"86400 s"`; where two adapters declare one type, the first by name is described. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  12. Projects: the list is in registration order with `available` (`false`, with `null` fields, for a folder that cannot be read or holds another id); `path` must be absolute and the folder missing or empty; a new project has `model_revision` 0 and `layout_version` 1 and no `index.sqlite` until its first run; opening a moved project re-points its registration, opening a copy whose original is still registered is E186; an unreadable `projects.json` is kept aside as `projects.json.unreadable` and the registry starts empty. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  13. The default `optimization` model is `variables: []`, `objective: {sense: "maximize", linear: {}}`, `constraints: []`; it validates today, and the owner's M5 item 14 (a model with no variables is an error, Phase 1a, AKS-130) will make it invalid, so that change must also change this default (for example a starter variable) and its test. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  14. `POST /v1/projects` with a `template` is 404 with E185 until the templates arrive (M10), and nothing is created. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
- **Spec sections updated:** spec 3 warning line, "Detailed specification" opening line, "Hardware tiers", "Service rules" (Token, Discovery, One service per user, Hardening, Lifetime, Client header), "Endpoints" (`domain` on `POST /v1/projects` and the notes on `system`, `engines`, `schema`, a component type, `templates`, the projects list, creation and open), "Status codes and errors", "Project folder"; dated line under "Changes in this version" (spec 3 stays 0.3 until the M6 close). Spec 1 "Validation issues" (E182 to E187) and "Engine adapter interface" (a component type's `description`), under a new "Since version 0.8".
- **Dependencies added:** the four in the entry above (`fastapi`, `uvicorn`, `websockets`; `httpx2` for development).
- **Review of PR #34 (Codex), first round, two findings, both real:**
  - Two starts at the same moment could both find no `service.json`, both serve on different ports, and the last writer would hide the other, with two registries writing one `projects.json`. Now a service holds an exclusive lock on `service.lock` in the configuration folder (the run lock's `take_lock`: `fcntl.flock`, or `msvcrt.locking` on Windows) from before it reads `service.json` until it exits. A start that cannot take the lock is the second start: it probes until the service answers (exit 0), for up to 10 s, twice spec 3's proposed 5-second readiness, then exits with status 1. Tests: `test_two_starts_at_once_give_one_service` (it failed with the lock stubbed out: both threads served and one crashed on the shared temporary file), `test_a_start_that_cannot_take_the_lock_and_finds_no_service_fails`, `test_the_lock_is_released_when_the_service_exits`. `main` takes `out` and `err` streams, so the threads in these tests do not share `sys.stdout`. Awaiting owner item 15: the lock file and the 10 s wait with exit status 1. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  - A relative `--data-dir` gave relative project paths in the API and in `projects.json`, which a service started from another folder could not find. `ServiceConfig` and `ProjectRegistry` now make both folders absolute, and `main` does too (`test_a_relative_data_folder_still_registers_absolute_paths`, `test_the_registry_makes_its_folders_absolute`).
  - Spec 3 "One service per user" and the `POST /v1/projects` note say so; the AKS-110 line under "Changes in this version" names both.
- **Review of PR #34 (Codex), second round, one finding, real:** `request_client` refused an `X-Client` other than `ui`, `mcp` or `script`, but no product route called it, so `POST /v1/projects` with `X-Client: browser` created a project. The `Hardening` middleware now checks the header for every HTTP request, after the token and before any route, and answers 400 with E184 (`test_every_product_route_refuses_another_x_client`, failing first on `GET /v1/system` and `POST /v1/projects`; `test_x_client_is_checked_after_the_token`). `request_client` keeps its own check for a route used without the middleware. Spec 3 "Client header" says where the check is made.
- **CI failure on PR #35, `checks (3.11)` (job 128454226900), real, in the AKS-110 code:** `test_python_dash_m_lcsim_service_runs_as_a_process` got "connection refused" just after reading `service.json`, on a runner about 2.3 times slower than a local run. The socket listens before `service.json` is written (`bind_local`), so a refused connection meant the service had already exited: `Lifetime` started its idle count when `create_app` built the application, before uvicorn had started, and with the test's 1.2 s limit (`--idle-minutes 0.02`) a slow start used the whole window, so the watcher called `on_idle` at its first check. Fixed in the product, not the test: the app's lifespan touches the lifetime when serving starts, so the idle count starts then at the earliest. Red test first: `test_the_idle_count_starts_when_the_service_starts_serving` (a fake clock moved past the limit between building the app and serving; the service called `on_idle` at once before the fix). `test_a_request_without_the_token_does_not_keep_the_service_up` now moves its clock after serving starts. Spec 3 "Lifetime" says so (owner to confirm, candidate bucket Phase 1a, with AKS-78).
- **Review of PR #34 (Codex), fourth round, one finding, real:** on Windows `os.path.isabs(r"\projects\demo")` is true although the path has no drive (`PureWindowsPath(...).is_absolute()` is false; checked with Python 3.11's `ntpath`), so `POST /v1/projects` and `POST /v1/projects/open` accepted and persisted a drive-relative path, which names a folder on whichever drive is current when the service starts. `projects.fully_qualified` now decides with the platform's `PurePath` flavour (`PATH_FLAVOUR`: `PureWindowsPath` on Windows, a drive and a root or a UNC share; `PurePosixPath` elsewhere), and both endpoints refuse anything else with E184 at `/path`. The other places AKS-110 takes or stores a path need no change: `--config-dir`, `--data-dir`, `ServiceConfig` and `ProjectRegistry` call `Path.absolute()`, which on Windows joins the current drive (and folder) to a drive-relative path, so they are fully qualified when stored; the registry stores only those folders and the endpoints' checked paths. Tests, failing first: `test_a_path_is_fully_qualified_only_with_its_drive_or_share` (Windows and POSIX flavours on any platform) and `test_both_project_endpoints_refuse_a_windows_path_without_drive_or_share` (with the Windows flavour, `\projects\demo` and `C:projects\demo`, nothing registered). Spec 3's notes on both endpoints say so (owner to confirm, candidate bucket Phase 1a).
- **Review of PR #34 (Codex), fifth round, one finding, real, the third in AKS-110's path handling (relative paths, drive-less Windows paths, now aliases), so it was redesigned rather than patched** (orchestrator note): `POST /v1/projects/open` compared folders lexically, so opening a registered project through a symbolic link or a Windows junction was refused as a copy (E186). The redesign is `src/lcsim/service/paths.py`: `fully_qualified` (the platform's `PurePath` flavour, moved from `projects.py`), `canonical` (absolute against the working folder, normalised, resolved to the real path with `Path.resolve`, which follows symbolic links and junctions and needs no existing path) and `same_folder` (equal canonical paths, or `os.path.samefile` when both exist, which also covers names that resolving leaves apart, such as a different letter case on Windows). `canonical` is used wherever a path is admitted or stored: the endpoints' `path` (after `fully_qualified`), `--config-dir` and `--data-dir` in `main`, `ServiceConfig`, `ProjectRegistry`'s folders, `create` and `open`; `same_folder` is the identity test in `open`, where a folder registered already under any name for it is returned unchanged, a different folder holding a registered id whose folder still holds it is still a copy (E186), and otherwise the registration moves (a moved folder). `create` needs no identity test, since it only uses missing or empty folders, and lookups are by id. Decision (rule 5, owner to confirm, candidate bucket Phase 1a): `projects.json` stores the canonical path, the folder itself rather than the name a client used, so a registration does not depend on an alias that may be removed, and the API shows that path. Tests, failing first where they could: `tests/service/test_paths.py` (flavours, canonical paths through a link and the working folder, `same_folder` by link, by `.`, for missing folders, and through `samefile`), `test_opening_a_registered_project_through_an_alias_is_the_same_project` (the finding: E186 before), `test_projects_json_stores_the_resolved_path`, `test_the_service_folders_are_resolved`, `test_a_copy_reached_through_an_alias_is_still_400_with_e186`, the existing move and copy tests, and the Windows-flavour endpoint test, now patching `lcsim.service.paths.PATH_FLAVOUR`. Symbolic-link tests are skipped where the system cannot make one. Spec 3's notes on `POST /v1/projects` and `POST /v1/projects/open` say so.
- **Review of PR #34 (Codex), sixth round, one finding, real, inside the path redesign:** `canonical` called `os.path.abspath`, which removes `..` by text, before `resolve()`, so `/work/alias/../project` with `alias` naming `/work/real/child` became `/work/project` instead of `/work/real/project`, the folder the file system means. `canonical` is now `Path(path).resolve()`, which makes the path absolute and follows each link before applying the `..` after it; the endpoints still check `fully_qualified` first. Red test first: `test_a_parent_step_after_an_alias_follows_the_alias` (skipped where links cannot be made). Spec 3's `POST /v1/projects` note says so (owner to confirm).

### Model endpoints, the sixteen edit kinds, revisions and project events (AKS-111)

- **Decision:**
  - **Layout.** `src/lcsim/model/edits.py` holds the sixteen edits as pure functions on a model document: `check_edits(edits)` returns the E184 issues of a batch's shape, and `apply_edits(model, edits, component_types)` returns the new model or one E180 issue at `/edits/<n>`, never changing the model it is given, so the MCP server (M7) and tests use it without the service. `src/lcsim/service/model_store.py` (`ModelStore`) reads, writes, edits and validates a project's model; `src/lcsim/service/events.py` (`ProjectEvents`) is the broadcaster; `src/lcsim/service/models.py` is the router of `GET`/`PUT .../model`, `POST .../model/edits`, `POST .../validate` and the `GET .../events` WebSocket, added to `ROUTERS`. `ServiceState` gains `models` and `events`.
  - **Writes.** Each project has a lock in the store, taken by every read and write of its model, so two writes stating the same `base_revision` cannot both pass the check (`test_two_writes_with_the_same_base_revision_cannot_both_pass` fails with the lock removed). A write saves `project.json` with the new `model_revision`, then `model.json`, each with `write_json` (a temporary file named for the process and thread, then `os.replace`; the temporary file is removed when the write fails). A write that fails is 400 with E186 and its exception goes to the log only. `write_json` refuses NaN and infinities (`allow_nan=False`), so no file the service writes can hold them.
  - **Events.** `ProjectEvents.listen(pid)` registers a listener in the WebSocket's event loop; `publish`, `model_written` and `run_changed` may be called from any thread and reach each listener through `call_soon_threadsafe`, in publishing order. The store publishes `model_written` while it holds the project's lock, so events go out in revision order. AKS-112 calls `state.events.run_changed(pid, run_id, state)` on every run state change. The route listens before it reads the revision, sends that revision first with `client` `null`, and leaves out a `model` event that revision already covers.
  - **The `domain` gap is closed without a new edit.** `POST /v1/projects` takes `domain` (AKS-110) and a whole-model `PUT` may change it (`test_put_may_change_the_domain`); no edit changes `domain`, because an edit that switched domain would leave every domain-specific key invalid at once. Spec 3 "Model edits" says so.
  - Not built here, as instructed: the owner's M5 decisions items 6 and 14.
- **Reason:** guide rules 5, 6 and the M6 row; spec 3 "Revision", "Endpoints", "Model edits", "Status codes and errors", "Canvas behaviour" ("External edits", "Undo and redo"); the AKS-111 acceptance list and its two comments (sixteen kinds with `set_label`; `set_path` and `remove_path` reaching `variables` and `objective`; the `domain` gap).
- **Alternatives considered:** a `{model, base_revision}` envelope for `PUT` (the body would no longer be the file, and a JSON envelope parsed by the framework hides a key written twice or `NaN`, which spec 1 makes E100); checking the schema after every edit (a batch that passes through an invalid state, such as removing `/meta` and setting it again, would be refused, and a 200-edit move would run 200 schema passes; the store checks once and replays only a failing batch to name the edit); rejecting every failing edit of a batch (a later edit often fails only because an earlier one did); E180 for a badly shaped edit (E184 is spec 3's code for a field missing, unknown or of the wrong kind, and these problems do not depend on the model); an `asyncio.Lock` per project (the routes that write run in the thread pool, and AKS-112's run driver publishes from its own threads); a `set_domain` edit (see above).
- **Awaiting owner** (rule 5, simplest readings; each written into spec 3, or spec 1, and marked owner to confirm):
  1. `PUT .../model` takes the model file itself as its body, whatever its content type, read as spec 1 reads a file; `base_revision` is the query parameter `?base_revision=N` (a whole number, else E184); the parsed file is saved as JSON indented by two spaces, not the bytes sent; a schema failure's `error.code` is its first issue's code (E100 or E101). Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  2. The issues of a write's response are loading's (schema and meaning passes, warnings included), not the adapter's `validate`, which `POST .../validate` adds. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  3. An edit is an object whose key `edit` names its kind; `id`, `new_id`, `type`, `from`, `to`, `experiment_id` are text, `params`, `ui`, `set` objects, `path` a JSON Pointer, `label` text or `null`, the rest any JSON value; an empty `edits`, an unknown kind or field, a missing field or a field of the wrong kind is 400 with E184 at `/edits/<n>` or `/edits/<n>/<field>`, every problem listed. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  4. Edits apply in order, each to the model the earlier ones left; only the first edit that cannot be applied is reported; an `id` names the first component, experiment or scenario with that id. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  5. Pointers address an array entry by an index without leading zeros; `set_setting` and `set_path` replace at an index and append at `-` or at the length; a `path` of `""` cannot be applied. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  6. `set_path` and `remove_path` reach `/meta`, `/run`, `/objectives`, `/constraints`, `/experiments/<n>/search` (spec 3 names `search`; it lives in an experiment, so it is addressed by the experiment's position), and in an `optimization` model `/variables` and `/objective`; another path, and a `remove_path` of a path that does not exist, cannot be applied (E180). Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  7. A batch whose result would fail the schema pass cannot be applied (E180 at the edit after the last one that left the model passing, with the first schema issue in the message), so `model.json` always passes it; examples are `set_path` of `/meta/name` to a number, and `connect` or `set_experiment` in an `optimization` model. Spec 3 listed only the cases where an edit cannot be performed at all; without this an edit could save a file that `PUT` would refuse. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  8. `set_scenario` naming an unknown parameter in `set` is E180 (spec 3's "an unknown parameter ... named by ... `set_scenario`"); `remove_scenario` of a scenario the experiment does not have is E180 too (spec 3 named only an unknown experiment; `remove_experiment` and `remove_parameter` already reject an unknown id). Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  9. `connect` and `disconnect` name ports, not ids, so they are never E180 for an unknown component or port (the meaning pass reports E130); `connect` leaves exactly one connection from its `from`; `disconnect` of a port with no connection changes nothing (and is still a write). Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  10. `remove_component` removes the connections at either end and leaves `pool`, `target` and metric references for the meaning pass (E124, E174); `rename_component` rewrites connection ends, every setting its type declares as naming a component (the catalogue's `refers_to`: `pool`, `target`) and every objective and constraint `metric` starting with `<id>.`. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  11. `remove_parameter` counts as references a `{"$param": id}` anywhere in a component's settings, a scenario `set` key and a `search.variables` entry; `set_parameter` and `set_experiment` never remove a field. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a (removing a field, such as a parameter's `max`: Phase 2, or drop?, since `PUT` can do it).
  12. A project whose `project.json` cannot be read, or whose `model.json` is missing or not JSON, is 400 with E186; edits on a `model.json` that fails the schema pass (changed outside the service) are E186 with its schema issues until a whole model is written with `PUT`. Spec 1's E186 row says so. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  13. `POST .../validate` returns `{revision, issues}`; the adapter's `validate` runs only when the schema and meaning passes find no error, since it needs a model that loads. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  14. The events WebSocket sends `{"type": "model", "revision": N, "client": null}` with the current revision after the token, then one event per write; an unknown project is closed with 1008 and the reason `E185` (an unreadable one with `E186`) after the token; what the client sends later is ignored. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  15. Writes are serialized per project inside the service process (one service per user, AKS-110), `project.json` before `model.json`; a 409 body has `issues` `[]`; a refused write is not a write and sends no event. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  16. A number JSON cannot hold once read, such as `1e400` (Python reads it as infinity), is E100 at its place in a `PUT` body and E184 in an edit's field, since it could not be saved; before this a `ui` holding one loaded with no issue, would have been written as `Infinity`, and every later read of the model would have failed with 500. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
- **Spec sections updated:** spec 3 warning line, "Detailed specification" opening line, "Revision", "Endpoints" (notes on the five model rows), "Model edits" (how edits are written and applied, and the `domain` gap), "Status codes and errors" (400 row and which bodies list issues); dated line under "Changes in this version" (spec 3 stays 0.3 until the M6 close). Spec 1 "Validation issues" (the E180 and E186 rows), dated line under "Since version 0.8".
- **Dependencies added:** none.
- **Review of PR #35 (Codex), first round, two findings, both real and of one class:** `remove_experiment` on a model without `experiments` (an optimization model) raised `KeyError`, and `remove_parameter` after a `set_experiment` that set a scenario's `set` to `null` raised `TypeError`; the API answered both with 500 instead of E180. The class: an edit walking an absent or malformed collection, in an intermediate state the batch is allowed to pass through or in a file changed outside the service, could raise. Every lookup in `src/lcsim/model/edits.py` now accepts any shape: a list or object an edit must change is created when absent and is E180 when of another kind (`_list`, `_mapping`), and scans read only objects in lists (`_objects`), skipping what they cannot read; `remove_experiment` finds the experiment before touching the list. A last guard in `apply_edits` turns any other exception into E180 at `/edits/<n>`, its details in the log only, so no edit can reach the API as 500. Tests: `test_no_edit_raises_on_a_model_of_any_shape` applies one plausible edit of each of the sixteen kinds to the shop model with each of 26 collections or entries absent, `null`, text, `[]`, `{}` or a list of non-objects, and the plan model likewise for `variables`, `objective`, `constraints` and `meta`, and asserts a clean application or one E180 (14 kinds failed before the change); `test_remove_experiment_in_an_optimization_model_is_e180`, `test_a_null_scenario_set_does_not_stop_removing_a_parameter`, `test_an_edit_that_fails_unexpectedly_is_e180_without_exception_text`, and the request tests `test_remove_experiment_in_an_optimization_model_is_e180_not_500` and `test_a_null_scenario_set_in_a_batch_does_not_fail_the_request`. Spec 3 "Model edits", Order, says so (owner to confirm, candidate bucket Phase 1a).
- **Review of PR #35 (Codex), second round, one finding, real:** a `model.json` changed outside the service to hold `1e400` (which Python reads as an infinity; `NaN` and `Infinity` literals too) passed the schema pass, so an edit reached `_save`, moved `model_revision` in `project.json`, and then raised `ValueError` from `write_json(..., allow_nan=False)`, giving 500 with the model unsaved; a `GET` failed while sending the body. Every way a model or a value enters was checked (orchestrator note): reading `model.json` (`GET`, edits, the 409 body, `validate`), a `PUT` body, an edit's fields, the other request bodies (`POST /v1/projects`, `POST /v1/projects/open`, `base_revision`), and opening an existing folder (whose `project.json` already must hold whole numbers, and whose `model.json` is read through the same path as any project's). Readings (owner to confirm, candidate bucket Phase 1a): on disk, `ModelStore._model` refuses such a file with 400 E186 and the E100 issue at the number's place (`non_finite_issue`), so edits and reads are refused before anything moves, and `validate` reports the E100 (spec 1's reading stage already reports the `NaN` and `Infinity` literals as E100 at `""`); in a `PUT` body it is E100 (as before, now through the same helper); in an edit's field E184 (`check_edits`, as before); in the other bodies the fields are text or whole numbers, so FastAPI's strict types already give E184. `_save` now makes both files into text (`json_text`) before writing either (`write_text`), so a value JSON cannot hold could never move the revision without the model. Tests, the disk ones failing first with 500: `test_a_model_json_on_disk_with_a_number_json_cannot_hold_is_e186` (`1e400`, `NaN`, `-Infinity`; `GET`, edits with and without `base_revision`, revision unchanged, `validate`, `PUT` repairs it), `test_an_opened_folder_whose_model_json_holds_one_is_e186_when_read`, `test_a_put_body_with_a_number_json_cannot_hold_is_e100`, `test_an_edit_value_with_a_number_json_cannot_hold_is_e184`, `test_other_bodies_with_a_number_json_cannot_hold_are_e184`. Spec 3 "Endpoints" notes and spec 1's E186 row say so.
- **Review of PR #35 (Codex), third round, two findings, both real, the third and fourth in one mechanism (how a model is admitted and saved), so it was redesigned rather than patched** (orchestrator note): `validate` skipped the non-finite check whenever loading reported anything, so a model that also warned (W202) was reported without its E100 and the adapter ran; and a lone surrogate (`"\ud800"`, which a JSON escape carries) in a `PUT` body or a `set_label` passed every check, then failed to encode as UTF-8 after `project.json` had been replaced: 500, revision moved, model unsaved, temporary file left. The redesign:
  - **One admission gate**, `model_store.admit(document)`: the exact bytes `model.json` would hold (`projects_store.encode_json`: `json.dumps` with `allow_nan=False`, then strict UTF-8), or an E100 at the first value that stops it (`lcsim.model.edits.first_unstorable`: a non-finite number, a string or key that is not valid Unicode). It runs unconditionally on every path: a `model.json` read from disk (`GET`, edits, the 409 body, an opened folder: E186 with the E100), a `PUT` body (E100), the result of an edit batch (E180 at `/edits`) and `validate` (the E100 alone, the adapter not called). `check_edits` reports such values in an edit's fields first, as E184, without quoting them; `POST /v1/projects` and `POST /v1/projects/open` refuse such text in their fields (E184). Every response is ASCII-escaped JSON (`errors.AsciiJSONResponse`, the app's default response class), as a last guard, so no text a client sent can stop a body from being sent.
  - **One save routine**, `ModelStore._commit`: both files' bytes are made first (a `project.json` whose own text cannot be written back is E186 before anything is written), each is written to a temporary file in the project folder, then `model.json` is renamed into place and `project.json` last, which is the commit; a failure before the commit removes the temporary files, and a failure at it also puts the previous model back (or removes a model that was not there before), so the revision, the model and the events are unchanged; the event is published only after the commit. This reverses the first AKS-111 order (`project.json` first): a service that stops between the two renames now leaves the new model under the old revision, which a later write's revision check does not reveal, where before it left a moved revision over the old model, which a client saw as a 409; the orchestrator's design was followed so that the revision never names a model that is not on disk. Owner to confirm the order; candidate bucket Phase 1a.
  - Tests, failing first (33 of them before the change): `tests/service/test_admission.py` feeds every admission path (disk read with `GET`, edits with and without `base_revision`, and `validate`; an opened folder; a `PUT` body; an edit's field) the values `1e400`, `NaN`, `Infinity`, `-Infinity` and `"\ud800"` in a label, a `ui` field, a setting and a `meta` string, each alone and in a model that warns (W202), and asserts a coded 4xx or a coded `validate` issue, an unchanged `model.json` and `project.json`, no file left in the folder and no event; it also checks a poisoned batch result (E180), a failing rename at each step (the old model put back), a commit that publishes only after `project.json` is renamed last, an unwritable `project.json` name (E186), a failed put-back (logged, still refused), and that `encode_json`, `write_json` and `AsciiJSONResponse` behave as described. Spec 3 "Revision" and "Endpoints" notes, and spec 1's E186 row, say so.
- **Review of PR #36 (Codex, AKS-112), the strict-reader class, fixed on this branch at the orchestrator's direction:** the run files of AKS-112 were read with `json.loads`, which takes `1e999` (an infinity), `NaN` and lone surrogates, so a hand-edited file could make a response fail with 500; the same class AKS-111 closed for `model.json`. Rather than a third local check, `src/lcsim/strict_json.py` now holds `encode_json` (moved from `projects_store`, which re-exports it) and `read_json`/`read_json_file`, the one strict reader: a document is read only when `encode_json` could write it back, else `UnreadableJSON` (a `ValueError`). `read_project` (`project.json`), `ProjectRegistry._load` (`projects.json`) and `active_tier` (`settings.json`, now written with `encode_json`) use it here; AKS-112 uses it for `manifest.json`, `summary.json` and its run settings. Tests, failing first: `tests/test_strict_json.py`; a `project.json` with a lone surrogate listed `available` `false` and E186 for its model; a `projects.json` with one set aside; a `settings.json` with one, `1e999` or `NaN` replaced with the detected tier (before, the first start raised while writing it back). `ModelStore._commit` no longer checks that `project.json` can be written back: the strict reader refuses such a file first, still with E186 (`test_a_project_json_whose_text_cannot_be_written_back_is_e186` passes unchanged). Owner to confirm; candidate bucket Phase 1a.
- **Review of PR #36 (Codex, AKS-112), third finding on numbers read from disk, so redesigned (orchestrator's direction), on this branch:** `lcsim.strict_json` adds `whole_in(value, minimum, maximum)` and `real_in(value, minimum, maximum, above_minimum=)`, the one typed, bounded parser for every number read from a file and then used. Each refuses a `bool`, text and any other kind, checks the range before any conversion (so `10**400` written out is refused, not passed to `float()`, which raised `OverflowError`), and never raises; `MAX_WHOLE` is 2⁶³ − 1, what every reader and SQLite can hold. Bounds here (rule 5, owner to confirm, candidate bucket Phase 1a): `project.json` `layout_version` 1 to `MAX_WHOLE` and `model_revision` 0 to `MAX_WHOLE`, otherwise unreadable (E186); a write that would pass `MAX_WHOLE` is E186 and saves nothing. Tests, failing first: `tests/test_strict_json.py` (both parsers over `10**400`, negatives, zero, floats, infinities, NaN, `bool`, text, lists, objects) and `test_a_project_json_number_out_of_its_range_is_unreadable`, `test_a_write_that_would_pass_the_largest_revision_is_400_with_e186`. AKS-112 applies the same parsers to `settings.json` and `manifest.json`.

### Run endpoints, the run queue, progress events, results and tables (AKS-112)

- **Decision:**
  - **Layout.** `src/lcsim/service/runs.py` is the router of the seven run rows of spec 3 (added to `ROUTERS`); `src/lcsim/service/run_service.py` (`RunService`, on `ServiceState.runs`) starts, finds, cancels and reads runs; `src/lcsim/service/run_feed.py` (`RunFeed`) holds each unfinished run's live state, latest progress and listeners; `src/lcsim/service/run_settings.py` reads the run settings; `src/lcsim/runs/queue.py` (`RunQueue`) and `src/lcsim/runs/index.py` (`RunIndex`, `index.sqlite`) are independent of the service. The local target is reused as it is, with additions: `LocalTarget` takes `on_state` and `on_progress` hooks (called under the run's lock after the manifest is written), `create` takes a `target` override, `LocalRun.cancel` returns whether it was accepted, `LocalRun.interrupt` stops a run and ends it `failed` with E191, and `check_experiment` raises `ExperimentChoiceError` (a `ValueError`, so earlier callers are unchanged) with its reason. `ModelStore.model_file` gives the admitted `model.json` bytes under the project's lock, so a run copies the file byte for byte and never a half-made write.
  - **Driving runs (rule 5, owner to confirm).** Spec 3 says runs are child processes of the service. Reading: the service drives each run from a daemon thread of its own (the run queue's), and the run's worker is a spawned child process of the service (spec 1 "Where the methods run"), so no client connection carries a run. The acceptance test `tests/service/test_run_disconnect.py` starts a real service process (`tests/service/gated_service.py`, the service with the test adapter `gated` installed), starts a run, drops the HTTP and WebSocket clients while the run is certainly unfinished (it waits for a `GO` file), lets it end, and finds it `done` with a full run folder through new connections.
  - **The service stopping (rule 5, owner to confirm).** Spec 3 says runs outlive the interface; spec 1 says what the next start does with orphaned runs, not what a stopping service does. Reading: runs do not outlive the service; when it stops (the lifespan's end) it starts no more runs, interrupts every unfinished run (the cancel grace applies) and marks each `failed` with E191, and waits up to 30 s for them; a service killed without stopping leaves that to the next start. A daemon thread is used so that a run whose worker will not end cannot keep a stopped service's process alive. The lifetime rule already keeps an idle exit from ever stopping a run, since a run holds the service from acceptance to its final state.
  - **Run queue (rule 5, owner to confirm).** One queue for the whole service, across projects; submissions are taken one at a time (`RunService._submitting`), so creation order, index order and queue order are the same, which is "arrival order"; `test_concurrent_requests_start_in_the_order_they_were_accepted` posts six runs from six threads and checks they start one at a time in `created` order and list in that order. `max_concurrent_runs`, `run_time_limit_s` and `run_memory_limit_bytes` are read from `settings.json` when the service starts (defaults 1, 600 and half the physical memory; a value of another kind takes its default and is logged); Settings writes them at M9.
  - **The index (rule 5, owner to confirm).** `index.sqlite` holds only what does not change (run id, `created`, `experiment_id`) in acceptance order; states are read from the manifests, the source of truth, so the cache can never disagree with them about a state. It is rebuilt when missing or unreadable (also a file of another layout), synced (missing folders added, gone ones dropped) the first time the service uses a project, and a run folder it lacks is found by a sync. A folder whose manifest cannot be read, has another `run_id`, `state` or `created`, or holds `NaN`, is not a run (`read_run_manifest`); `recover_interrupted` used to raise on such a folder, which would have stopped the service from starting (fixed here, spec 1 line).
  - **Recovery (rule 5, owner to confirm).** On start, for every registered project, and for a project opened later the first time its runs are used, orphaned runs are failed with E191 and announced as project `run` events.
  - **Responses (rule 5, owner to confirm).** A run is `{run_id, project_id, experiment_id, state, fraction, message, created, started, ended, error}`; `message` is the latest progress message kept in memory, `""` when unknown (the manifest does not keep it, spec 1). Cancel: a queued run moves to `cancelled` at once, a running one is accepted and stays `running` until its worker stops, a final one is 409 with E188 (new spec 1 code: spec 3's 409 for runs had none). Summary: `{summary, findings}` with `findings` `[]` until M8; a run not `done` is 409 with E188 for the summary and tables.
