Tables: `format` defaults to `json`, one object per row with NaN as `null`; `csv` with a header row, NaN as an empty field and floats as `repr` (shortest round trip); another format is 400 with E184; an unknown name, a table the run lacks (every optimization run) or an unreadable one is 404 with E185. A `summary.json` holding `NaN` (changed outside the product) is 404, not 500.
  - **Starting (rule 5, owner to confirm).** `POST .../runs` takes `{experiment_id?, target?}`, both text, and no body as `{}`. Checks in the target's order: loading (422, the first error's code in `error.code`, every issue listed), the target (the request's when given; E172 at `/target` for the request's, at `/run/target` for the file's), the experiment (400 with E184 at `/experiment_id`: none for a process model, one for an optimization model, or an unknown one), the adapter's `validate` and the fidelity check (422). Nothing is created when a check fails. The manifest's `target` is the target used, `project_id` the project's id and `client` the `X-Client`.
  - **Progress events (rule 5, owner to confirm).** After the token: an unknown run closes with 1008 and `E185`; then `state` and `progress` from one snapshot taken under the feed's lock together with the listener's registration, so no change is lost or sent twice (`test_listeners_racing_a_publisher_see_each_change_exactly_once`); then each change; after the final state a close with 1000. A run already final (or not driven by this service) sends its manifest's state and progress and closes. Search runs send the search's weighted fraction and its "Search, candidate n" and "Confirmation, candidate n" messages (`test_a_search_runs_through_the_api_and_its_progress_messages_reach_the_stream`). `metrics` events are not sent: the worker's pipe carries fraction and message only, and no Phase 1 adapter reports live metrics.
  - **A driver that fails (rule 5, owner to confirm).** An exception in the process driving a `running` run that is not the adapter's (a state or progress hook, a log write) now sets the token and fails the run with E190, the traceback in `log.txt`, instead of leaving it `running` with the service held for ever; spec 1's E190 rule says so (`test_an_unexpected_failure_while_driving_a_run_fails_it_with_e190`). Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
  - Not built here, as instructed: the owner's M5 decisions items 6 and 14.
- **Reason:** guide rules 5, 6 and the M6 row; spec 3 "Service rules" (Lifetime, Runs outlive the interface, Run queue, Client header), "Endpoints", "Status codes and errors", "Progress events", "Project folder"; spec 1 "Run targets", "Run folder and results"; spec 4 "Limits"; the AKS-112 acceptance list and its re-check of 2026-11-07.
- **Alternatives considered:** keeping each run's state in `index.sqlite` (two copies of the state that a crash between the writes could make disagree; reading a manifest per run is cheap at Phase 1 sizes); ordering the queue by `run_id` (its time has one-second resolution, so two runs in one second would start in random order); letting a stopping service leave its runs to the next start (the process would wait for non-daemon workers at exit while no longer serving, and a second service could start beside it); marking them `cancelled` (nobody cancelled them; E191 is spec 1's code for a run interrupted by the service); answering a cancel of a running run only once it has stopped (it can take the 5 s grace and more; the client follows the events); a project-level `metrics` relay (no adapter reports metrics, so it could not be tested).
- **Awaiting owner** (rule 5, simplest readings; each written into spec 3 or spec 1 and marked owner to confirm; candidate buckets in brackets):
  1. Runs are driven from service threads with child worker processes; a stopping service fails its unfinished runs with E191 (Phase 1a).
  2. One run queue across projects; arrival order is acceptance order; the three run settings are read from `settings.json` at start, with defaults (Phase 1a; the Settings screen at M9).
  3. `index.sqlite` holds run id, `created` and `experiment_id` only; states come from manifests; rebuilt and synced as above; folders that are not runs are skipped (Phase 1a).
  4. The run view's fields and `message` `""` when unknown (Phase 1a).
  5. Cancel semantics and E188 for 409 (Phase 1a).
  6. `{summary, findings}` and 409 for a run not `done` (Phase 1a).
  7. Table formats: JSON rows with `null` for NaN, CSV with empty fields; `json` by default (Phase 1a).
  8. The order of the `POST .../runs` checks, E172 at `/target`, and no body as `{}` (Phase 1a).
  9. The events stream's snapshot, close codes (1000 after the final state, 1008 with `E185`), and no `metrics` events until an adapter reports live metrics (Phase 1a for the run monitor of M9; carrying `Progress.metrics` through the worker's pipe is Phase 2 unless the owner wants live key numbers at M9).
  10. Project `run` events: `queued` at creation, then every change, and `failed` for E191 recoveries (Phase 1a).
- **Spec sections updated:** spec 3 warning line, "Detailed specification" opening line, "Lifetime", "Runs outlive the interface", "Run queue", "Endpoints" (the projects-events, `POST /v1/projects` and run notes), "Status codes and errors" (409, `error.code`), "Progress events", "Project folder"; dated line under "Changes in this version" (spec 3 stays 0.3 until the M6 close). Spec 1 "Validation issues" (E188), "Run folder and results" (E191 when the service stops, recovery on first use, folders that are not runs), "Manifest values" (`project_id`, `client`, `target`); dated line under "Since version 0.8".
- **Dependencies added:** none (`websockets` 17.4, BSD-3-Clause, was already a dependency through AKS-110; the disconnect test uses its client).
- **Review of PR #36 (Codex), first round, one finding, real:** when a progress hook raised while `_watch` was reading an active worker, the run was failed at once but its worker was neither stopped nor joined, so a worker that ignores cancel (`StubbornAdapter`) went on after the run's queue place and lifetime hold were released. `_run_worker` now catches a failure of `_watch`, sets the token, gives the worker the limit grace and ends it with the processes it started (`_stop`), then lets the failure reach the E190 guard. Test: `test_a_driver_failure_stops_and_reaps_a_worker_that_ignores_cancel` (failed before: the worker was still alive).
- **Review of PR #36 (Codex), second round, one finding, real; the class was redesigned at the orchestrator's direction:** `read_run_manifest` and the summary reader refused only the `NaN` and `Infinity` literals, so `1e999` (read as an infinity) or a lone surrogate in a hand-edited `manifest.json` or `summary.json` made the run detail, run list or summary answer 500 when the response was encoded. Instead of a third local check, every JSON file the service reads back now goes through `lcsim.strict_json.read_json` (added on AKS-111's branch with `encode_json` and merged up, see that entry): `manifest.json` (a run it refuses is not a run: 404 with E185 on every run route and left out of the list), `summary.json` (404 with E185) and the run settings (every default). Tests, failing first: `test_a_manifest_that_cannot_be_written_back_is_404_everywhere_not_500` and `test_a_summary_that_cannot_be_written_back_is_404_not_500` for `1e999`, `-1e999`, `NaN`, `-Infinity` and `"\ud800"` over run detail, cancel, summary, both table formats and the list; `test_a_settings_file_that_cannot_be_written_back_gives_the_defaults`. Tables are Parquet, read by pyarrow, whose strings are UTF-8 and whose non-finite floats are already `null` or empty.
- **Review of PR #36 (Codex), third round, one finding, real; the third in "numbers read from disk", so redesigned (orchestrator's direction):** `10**400` written out in `settings.json` passed the strict reader and `_positive`, then `float()` raised `OverflowError` in `create_app`, so the service could not start. Every number the service reads from a file and uses now goes through the shared typed, bounded parsers `whole_in` and `real_in` (`lcsim.strict_json`, added on AKS-111's branch, afe6acc, merged up), which check kind and range before any conversion and never raise. Bounds (rule 5, owner to confirm, candidate bucket Phase 1a; spec 3 "Run queue", spec 1 "Run folder and results"): `max_concurrent_runs` 1 to 64, `run_time_limit_s` above 0 and at most 7 days, `run_memory_limit_bytes` 1 to 2⁶³ − 1, each otherwise logged and defaulted; a manifest is a run only with `fraction` 0 to 1, `seed` `null` or 0 to 2⁶³ − 1, and `project_id`, `experiment_id`, `started`, `ended` text or `null` (otherwise 404 with E185 and left out of the list); `project.json` as in the AKS-111 entry. Tests, failing first: `test_a_whole_setting_of_another_kind_or_out_of_range_takes_its_default`, `test_a_time_limit_of_another_kind_or_out_of_range_takes_its_default`, `test_the_bounds_themselves_are_taken`, `test_the_service_starts_with_a_settings_file_holding_huge_numbers`, `test_a_manifest_field_of_another_kind_or_out_of_range_is_404` (`10**400`, negatives, zero, floats where a whole number is needed, `bool`, text). `test_a_huge_time_limit_is_kept` became `test_a_huge_time_limit_takes_the_default` (1e300 s is above the bound).
- **CI on PR #36 (`checks (3.11)` at 01df329), one failure, root-caused:** `test_events_close_with_1008_when_the_run_vanishes_after_it_was_found` did not raise. The `done_run` fixture waited until `GET /v1/runs/{rid}` said `done`, which is read from the manifest, but the run target writes the manifest before it calls the state hook that drops the run from the live feed. On the slower runner the WebSocket opened in that gap, took the live `running` snapshot, and closed with 1000 after the final event, so the path the test targets was never reached. The product behaves as specified (the final state is sent exactly once), and a run deleted between lookup and stream start still closes with 1008: a live run streams from memory, and one that is not live reads its manifest, whose refusal is the 1008 path. The fixture now also waits until the run has left the feed. `slow_final` widens the gap by 1 s to make the failure deterministic: `test_done_run_is_not_live_even_when_the_run_leaves_the_feed_slowly` failed without the fixture change, and `test_a_stream_opened_while_a_run_is_ending_sends_done_exactly_once` covers a stream opened inside the gap. Spec 3 "Progress events" now describes the window. No sleeps or retries were added to the product or to the waits.
- **CI on PR #36 (`checks (3.13)` at f9c9bbc), the second timing failure in "when is a run finished", so redesigned (orchestrator's direction; rule 5, owner to confirm, candidate bucket Phase 1a):** `test_a_run_is_accepted_with_202_and_reaches_done_with_a_full_run_folder` saw `done` while `worker.lock` still existed. `GET /v1/runs/{rid}` read the final manifest, which the run target writes before it removes the lock (E191 recovery needs that order). There is now one finishing order. On disk: tables and summary, then the final manifest, then the lock removed. The service publishes a final state (feed, run events, project `run` events, the lifetime hold released) only after that. For a run the queue executed, it also waits for the queue's `on_finished` report, which comes once the run's place is free; a queued run that ends without starting is published at once. The run endpoints show a run still in the feed as last published (state, fraction and message from the feed, no `ended` or `error`), and the summary and tables follow that state. Tests, failing first, with `slow_unlock` (1 s before the lock is removed): `test_a_final_state_is_visible_only_once_the_run_has_finished` (`done` and `cancelled`, across the run endpoint, the run's events and the project's events: no lock, a free queue, the run gone from the feed), `test_a_finishing_run_is_shown_as_it_was_until_it_has_finished`, and `test_a_stream_opened_while_a_run_is_finishing_sends_done_exactly_once`. The earlier fixture-only fix (f9c9bbc) is kept as a check. Spec 1 "Finishing order" and spec 3 "Progress events" say so.

### Data import endpoints, the column index, the distribution fit and `empirical` `data` (AKS-113)

- **Decision:**
  - **Research first.** `docs/research/spec-3-distribution-fit.md` (guide rule 3). The fit library is `scipy.stats` (already a dependency, BSD-3-Clause), by maximum likelihood; the fit score is the Kolmogorov-Smirnov statistic (`scipy.stats.kstest`), from 0 (best) to 1, used to rank. Rule 7: the product writes no estimator, optimiser or iteration; it chooses the families that apply, divides the column by its largest absolute value before the fit and multiplies location and scale back (exact for these families; without it the Weibull and normal fits of columns near 10⁻³⁰⁰ or 10³⁰⁰ were wrong or infinite), maps scipy's parameters to spec 1's fields (the lognormal's mean and sd by closed-form formulas), and leaves out a family whose fit raises or whose fields spec 1 would refuse (E160). Uploads go through Starlette's `MultiPartParser` on `python-multipart`; Excel workbooks are read with `openpyxl` with `defusedxml` installed.
  - **Layout.** `src/lcsim/model/data_files.py` reads CSV or Excel bytes into columns (names, types, float64 numbers, record count, preview) and holds the file-naming rule; every content problem is `UnreadableData` (E189), and every exception from the CSV reader or openpyxl is caught there. `src/lcsim/model/data.py` gains each column's `type`, each file's `format`, `sheet`, `size`, `rows` and `imported`, and the column index file `data/index.json` (`index_bytes`, `read_data_index`, strict reader, every value of a declared kind and range; an index that cannot be read is empty). `src/lcsim/model/fit.py` is the fit. `src/lcsim/runs/snapshots.py` reads a run's `data` columns, writes the snapshots and gives the model with the values. `src/lcsim/service/data_store.py` (`DataStore`, on `ServiceState.data`) and the router `src/lcsim/service/data.py` (added to `ROUTERS`) are the four data endpoints. `src/lcsim/service/commit.py` (`replace_in_order`) is the AKS-111 commit pattern made shared: the model store's `_commit` now uses it (its tests pass unchanged), and an import saves the file then the index (the commit). `ModelStore` reads the project's column index by default (the `no_data` hook is gone), its per-project lock is re-entrant and public (`locked`), and it loads every model, which hashes the data files, under that lock.
  - **Consistency.** An import saves the file and its index entry under the project's write lock, and validation, model writes and a run's creation read the index and hash the files under the same lock, so none meets a file renamed before its index is committed (`test_validation_hashes_the_data_files_while_the_project_s_writes_are_held` failed without the lock). A run also hashes the very bytes it reads the column from, so a file replaced after validation is E162, never copied under the old hash.
  - **Spec 1 inconsistency resolved first** (rule 6, ticket): `data_files` is one entry per `data` reference, `{component, setting, file, column, sha256}`, in "Run folder and results" and "Distributions".
  - **The two-distributions case.** A `failure` block with both `time_to_failure` and `time_to_repair` from one file's two columns keeps two snapshots, `breakdown.time_to_failure.parquet` and `breakdown.time_to_repair.parquet`, two `data_files` entries, and runs to `done` (`tests/runs/test_snapshots.py`, `tests/service/test_data.py`).
  - **The sampling test** of spec 1 for `empirical` with `data` runs against the copied column in seconds (mean within four standard errors of the copied column's mean, the standard error from its population sd, 20,000 samples from one stream of the spec 1 scheme), closing the Build guide's known gap.
  - **`DataNotYetSupportedError` is now `DataNotReadError`** (E162): a `data` distribution whose column a run has not read cannot be sampled or translated; one a run has read samples its values like `values`.
- **Reason:** guide rules 3, 5, 6, 7 and the M6 row; the known gap "`empirical` with `data` has never run" (M6, with the import endpoint); spec 3 "Endpoints" (the four data rows), "Project folder", "Screens" (Data); spec 1 "Distributions", "Random streams", "Run folder and results"; the AKS-113 acceptance list and both comments.
- **Alternatives considered:** Akaike's criterion as the score (meaningless on its own, depends on the unit, and undefined for a uniform or triangular fit whose support touches the data); method of moments (also iterates in scipy for most families, and can leave data outside the support); `python-calamine` for Excel (fast and also reads `.xls`, but a compiled Rust package, where openpyxl and its dependency are pure Python); reading the whole request with `request.form()` (Starlette spools any size to disk; the stream is capped first instead); a header convention such as `gap (min)` for units (one more parsing rule; the `units` part is explicit); refusing to delete a file the model names (the model stays editable, and validation names each reference); adding a new code for content problems as E184 (E184 is a malformed request; a well-formed request with a file that cannot be used is a different problem, so E189); storing the snapshots in seconds (a copy should hold the column as the file does; the unit is a column, as in the results tables).
- **Awaiting owner** (rule 5, simplest readings; each written into spec 3 or spec 1 and marked owner to confirm; candidate buckets in brackets):
  1. The upload form: the part `file` and an optional `units` JSON object of Phase 1 time units or `null`; units are declared only at import, so changing one means importing the file again (Phase 1a; a units edit without re-upload: Phase 2 or drop?).
  2. The file-naming rule: 1 to 100 ASCII letters, digits, space, `.`, `_`, `-`, a letter or digit first, `.csv` or `.xlsx`, no Windows device name; a name differing from an indexed one only in case is E184; the same name imports again in place; a Windows path sent as the name is reduced to its last part by `python-multipart` (Phase 1a; non-ASCII names: Phase 2).
  3. Formats: CSV in UTF-8 with commas only; `.xlsx` from its first worksheet with saved values; no `.xls`, `.xlsm`, other encodings or semicolons (Phase 1a; other encodings, delimiters and a sheet choice: Phase 2).
  4. Limits: 64 MiB per file (plus 64 KiB of form), 512 MiB unpacked, 1,048,576 records, 10,000,000 cells, 200-character column names, a 10-record preview (Phase 1a, with the perf-M6 measurement).
  5. Cells and types: a number is finite and in spec 1's number grammar; empty cells are not values; an all-empty row is skipped; a non-empty cell beyond the header is E189; a column is `number`, `text` or `empty` (Phase 1a).
  6. The column index `data/index.json` (`index_version` 1) and its strict reading; an index that cannot be read is taken as empty, so every reference is E162 until the files are imported again (Phase 1a).
  7. E162 also covers a column that is not `number`, or has no unit where a time is taken (Phase 1a).
  8. New spec 1 code E189 (400) for a data file that cannot be imported or a column that cannot be fitted (Phase 1a).
  9. A file the model names may be removed; validation then reports E162 (Phase 1a).
  10. The fit: the families tried and when, `constant` alone for a constant column, no `empirical` candidate, the Kolmogorov-Smirnov score, best first; fields as quantities in the column's unit (Phase 1a).
  11. The snapshot's columns (`value` as in the file, `unit`) and the `data_files` entry `{component, setting, file, column, sha256}` (Phase 1a).
  12. The responses: the upload's entry with `preview`, the list as an array, DELETE returning the remaining files, the fit's `{file, column, unit, values, score, candidates}` (Phase 1a).
- **Spec sections updated:** spec 3 warning line, "Detailed specification" opening line, "Endpoints" (the fit row, notes on the four data endpoints, the `validate` note), "Status codes and errors" (E189 for 400, data files for 404, `error.code`), "Project folder" (the column index); dated line under "Changes in this version" (spec 3 stays 0.3 until the M6 close). Spec 1 warning line, "Distributions" (the `empirical` row, how a column is read and sampled, E162's cases, the sampling test), "Validation issues" (E162 row, new E189), "Run folder and results" (`data_files`); dated line under "Since version 0.8".
- **Dependencies added** (versions installed on 2026-11-07; licences read from each installed distribution's licence file, on Python 3.13.18 in the project venv and 3.11.18 in a fresh scratch venv, with the same versions on both; PyPI JSON read for the upload dates and files; not legal advice, AKS-83 deferred):
  - `python-multipart>=0.0.33` (0.0.33, uploaded 2026-10-31), Apache-2.0 (`python_multipart-0.0.33.dist-info/licenses/LICENSE.txt`, the Apache License 2.0 text; licence expression `Apache-2.0`), confirming AKS-110's research; pure Python, `requires_python >=3.11`; no runtime dependencies. Used through Starlette's `MultiPartParser`.
  - `openpyxl>=3.1.5` (3.1.5, uploaded 2024-06-28, the latest), MIT (`openpyxl-3.1.5.dist-info/LICENCE.rst`, "Copyright (c) 2010 openpyxl"); pure Python; its one runtime dependency `et-xmlfile` 2.0.0, MIT (`LICENCE.rst`, "Copyright (c) 2010 openpyxl", with `LICENCE.python` for the standard-library code it includes). openpyxl ships no `py.typed`: a mypy override, as for scipy and pyomo, with the one use typed by a Protocol in `data_files.py`.
  - `defusedxml>=0.7.1` (0.7.1, uploaded 2021-03-08, the latest), PSF-2.0 (`defusedxml-0.7.1.dist-info/LICENSE`, "Python Software Foundation License Version 2"); pure Python; no runtime dependencies. openpyxl uses it when installed (`openpyxl.DEFUSEDXML` is `True`), refusing entity declarations; openpyxl's documentation recommends it, and the Python documentation says Expat below 2.7.2 may be open to the billion laughs and quadratic blowup attacks (the scratch 3.11.18 has Expat 2.6.1).
  - Every licence is permissive (Apache-2.0, MIT, PSF-2.0) and compatible with AGPL-3.0 for a combined work; all three are pure Python, so they install the same way on Windows.
- **Review of PR #37 (Codex), first round, one finding, real:** a column of finite values with a declared unit, such as `1e308` in minutes, passed import and validation, but its conversion to seconds gave an infinity, so the run was created and queued and failed only when `write_input` refused to write it; inline quantities are refused for this (E141, spec 1 "Quantities"). Fixed at the orchestrator's direction where the reference is validated, with the check inline quantities use: `lcsim.model.data.fits_in_seconds` parses the value and unit with `parse_quantity`; the column index records each `number` column's `largest` absolute value (read back with `real_in`), the meaning pass reports E162 at `column` when `largest` does not fit (so `PUT`, `validate` and `POST .../runs` report it, 422, before any run exists), the run checks the values it copies too (an index edited by hand cannot let an infinity in), and the fit leaves out a candidate whose time field would not fit. Rule 5 (owner to confirm, candidate bucket Phase 1a): the code is E162, spec 1's code for a data reference that cannot be used, rather than E141, which names a quantity written in the model. Red tests first: `test_a_column_that_overflows_in_seconds_is_refused_by_validate_and_by_runs`, `test_a_column_whose_values_overflow_in_seconds_is_e162_at_column`, `test_values_that_overflow_in_seconds_are_refused_even_when_the_index_says_otherwise`, `test_a_field_that_would_overflow_in_seconds_is_left_out`, and the index tests for `largest`. Spec 1 "Distributions" and spec 3 "Endpoints" and "Project folder" say so, with dated lines.
- **Review of PR #37 (Codex), second round, one finding, real, in the same mechanism (the fit's checks):** the constant-column path returned early without the checks, so a column of `1e308` minutes gave the candidate `"1e+308 min"`, which the model refuses (E141). Redesigned at the orchestrator's direction rather than patched: `lcsim.model.fit._admitted` is one final gate that every candidate passes, whatever path made it. It writes the candidate into a minimal model as a source's `interarrival` and loads it with the model layer's `load_document`, so the parser and meaning-pass rules the candidate would meet in the user's model decide (E123, E140, E141 for each field, E160 for the fields that must agree). The separate `_agrees` checks are gone. A candidate of a column without a unit is checked with its plain numbers read as seconds. Red test first: `test_a_constant_column_that_overflows_in_seconds_gives_no_candidate`; also `test_no_family_survives_when_every_field_would_overflow_in_seconds` and the property test `test_every_candidate_validates_when_written_into_a_model` (eight columns, four units: every candidate loads with no E123, E140, E141 or E16x). Spec 3's fit note says so.

### Route table test, request coverage, offline test and perf-M6 (AKS-114)

- **Decision:**
  - **Route table test** (spec 3 acceptance "Route table test", M6; guide M6 "Done when"). `tests/service/test_routes.py` reads the "Endpoints" table from `docs/specs/3-desktop-app.md` when it runs (rows whose first cell is a method and a path in backquotes, between "### Endpoints" and the next heading; 25 rows, none twice) and asserts it equals `lcsim.service.app.route_table(app)` both ways, each direction a test of its own so a failure names the extra or missing route. It was seen failing with a row removed from and a row added to the spec. `tests/service/test_app.py` keeps its per-ticket route sets.
  - **Every row has a request test** (rule 5, owner to confirm, candidate bucket Phase 1a). A request test is a request in `tests/service/` that the router matched to the row's route with the row's method. `tests/service/route_recorder.py` wraps the application that the transport of every Starlette `TestClient` calls (`client.app` stays the `FastAPI` app, which several tests check) and records `scope["route"].path`, the full template, with the method, or `GET` for a WebSocket route. A request refused by the hardening middleware, one to an unknown path and one with another method are not counted. `tests/service/conftest.py` installs it for the session, moves the coverage test to the end of the session, and notes when a service test module was not collected or a service test was deselected; the coverage test then runs `tests/service` in a separate pytest process (about 75 s) that writes its record to a file, so a partial run (one file, `-k`) never decides it. It fails rather than passes when nothing is recorded (were FastAPI or Starlette to change what the record reads), and was seen failing with one route's record suppressed (`DELETE /v1/projects/{pid}/data/{file}` named). Alternatives considered: a static scan of the test sources for paths (a path written in a test is not a request that reached the route, and f-strings with ids are hard to match); a middleware in the product (test needs in shipped code); a `pytest_sessionfinish` check (a failure there is not a test failure).
  - **Offline test** (spec 3 acceptance "The app works with outbound network blocked", M6; spec 0 principle 6; rule 5, owner to confirm, candidate bucket Phase 1a). "Outbound network blocked" is read as: every connection, datagram and name lookup that is not loopback is refused in the service and in every process it starts for a run. `tests/service/test_offline.py` starts a real service process through `tests/service/offline_service.py`, which installs the guard of `tests/service/offline_guard.py` at import when `LCSIM_TEST_OFFLINE_DIR` is set: an audit hook (PEP 578) refusing `socket.connect`, `socket.sendto` and `socket.sendmsg` to a non-loopback address and `getaddrinfo`, `gethostbyname`, `gethostbyname_ex` and `gethostbyaddr` of a name other than `localhost` or a loopback address, raising `OSError` and writing each attempt to a file. Run workers are spawned and import the launcher again as their main module, so they install the guard too; each process writes `armed-<pid>`. Over loopback HTTP the test creates a project, writes the coffee shop, edits its experiment (2 h, 2 replications), validates, runs, and reads the summary and both tables (JSON and CSV), then asserts the guard was on in the service and at least one worker, and that no process attempted anything. Seen failing with a connection attempt added to the launcher's worker path (three processes logged it). Two more tests show, in fresh processes, that the guard refuses a connect, a lookup and a datagram and allows loopback, and that the launcher arms it only with the variable set and starts no service as `__mp_main__`. An audit hook was chosen over monkeypatching `socket.socket` (which the instructions name) because it holds in spawned processes and for every library, and cannot be bypassed by a module that kept a reference to the original function; it cannot be removed, so it is installed only in processes made for the test, never in the pytest process and never in `src/lcsim`. The test client (the pytest process) is not guarded: it is the user, not the app.
  - **perf-M6** (guide protocol; spec 3 "Performance targets"; rule 5, owner to confirm, candidate bucket Phase 1a). `tests/perf/test_service.py` (marker `perf`, out of `check.sh`) measures a real `python -m lcsim.service`: ready (first 200 from `GET /v1/system`, three starts), `POST .../validate` of a 200-component model (a source, 198 servers, a sink) over loopback HTTP, the largest of 20 calls, and idle memory (the service and its processes from `/proc`, after 5 s without a request, after the start and after the coffee shop's 30-replication run), with 300 MB read as 300,000,000 bytes. It also measures the machine-count search through the local target as shipped (budget 10) and at the default budget of 50 with `tills` widened to 1 to 50 (so the exhaustive path searches 50 candidates; with 1 to 5 it would stop at five), and `fit_column` on deterministic triangular columns of 1,000 to 1,048,576 values (the import's record limit), each family timed alone. Results, on 4 cores under `taskset -c 0-3` (`docs/research/perf-M6.md`; Python 3.13.18, 3.11.18 in brackets): ready 1.33 to 1.38 s (1.39 to 1.45 s) against 5 s; validation median 0.076 s, max 0.160 s (0.080, 0.156) against 0.2 s; idle 178 MiB after the start (185) and 249 MiB, 261 MB, after a run (257 MiB, 269 MB) against 300 MB; search 7.6 s for 110 replications (7.9) and 51.9 s for 560 (54.3), best `tills` 2 both times; fit 20.9 s at the record limit (23.0), 18.5 s of it the triangular fit. All three targets measured and met; owner to confirm on the low tier (AKS-82).
  - **Spec 3 to 0.4** (guide rule 6; the milestone closes with changes to spec 3). The lines added after 0.3 (from the review of PR #1 on 2026-11-06 to the M6 build) sit under a new line "2026-11-07, version 0.4, M6 closed (AKS-114)", as spec 1 did for 0.4; the warning line and the "Detailed specification" opening line say the service and API are built; "Endpoints" says how the route table test reads the table and what a request test is; "Performance targets" says how the three service targets are measured and gives the M6 figures; the acceptance table names the evidence of the three M6 rows.
- **Reason:** guide rules 2, 4, 5, 6, 8, 9 and 10, the M6 row and the "Performance measurement protocol"; spec 3 "Endpoints", "Performance targets" and its acceptance table; spec 0 principle 6; M5 "Left", "Search cost"; the AKS-113 note on the fit near the record limit; the AKS-114 acceptance list.
- **Alternatives considered:** see each item above. For the perf checks: timing validation in-process (smaller, but not what a client waits for); one start only (the first start writes the tier, later starts read it, so both are timed); the search through `POST .../runs` (the same local target; the service adds only the queue).
- **Spec sections updated:** spec 3 version line, warning line, "Detailed specification" opening line, the note after the "Endpoints" table, "Performance targets", acceptance table (evidence of the M6 rows), "Changes in this version" (the 0.4 line and a dated AKS-114 line).
- **Dependencies added:** none. Memory is read with `lcsim.runs.limits.process_tree_memory` and `resource.getrusage` (guarded for Windows by the existing `max_rss_bytes`); the HTTP client is `httpx2`, already a development dependency.

### M6 closed (AKS-114)

- **Decision:**
  - M6 is closed. Every gate in the guide's M6 "Done when" row and every spec acceptance row tagged M6 passes on the top of the M6 stack (PRs #34, #35, #36, #37 and this change). Evidence is in `docs/sessions/2026-11-07-M6.md`:
    - `tests/service/test_routes.py` passes: the app's route set equals spec 3's 25-row endpoint table both ways;
    - every endpoint has a request test: `test_every_row_of_the_endpoint_table_has_a_request_test` finds every row reached by a request that the router matched;
    - a run survives a client disconnect: `tests/service/test_run_disconnect.py::test_a_run_survives_a_client_disconnect` (AKS-112);
    - the app works with outbound network blocked: `tests/service/test_offline.py::test_the_app_works_with_outbound_network_blocked`;
    - `docs/research/perf-M6.md` exists: the three service targets measured and met on 4 cores, owner to confirm on the low tier (AKS-82);
    - `pytest -m benchmark` 18 passed (5 M2, 6 M4 and 5 M5 cases, and the two machine-count tests);
    - `bash scripts/check.sh` exits 0 on Python 3.13.18 and 3.11.18 with 3,117 passed and 26 deselected (16 `benchmark_coverage`, 10 `perf`) and line coverage of `src/lcsim` 100% (8,091 statements); `tests/test_docs.py` passes.
  - The M6 research items have their notes: spec 3 "Framework check" (service half, `docs/research/spec-3-service-framework-check.md`, AKS-110) and the distribution fit (`docs/research/spec-3-distribution-fit.md`, AKS-113). The interface half of the framework check was done for M9 on 2026-11-06.
  - The known-gaps rows assigned to M6 have their outcomes. "The API, model edits and progress events have never run": they have run, and spec 3 was changed first wherever the build found it unclear or awkward (dated lines under "Changes in this version", AKS-110 to AKS-114). "`empirical` with `data` has never run": it passes the sampling test against the copied column (AKS-113). "Interface and service frameworks were not re-checked": the service half is done (FastAPI, uvicorn with websockets; AKS-110); the interface half was done on 2026-11-06 and is re-checked at M9 as the row says. "Performance targets are proposals": the spec 3 service targets are measured at M6 (`docs/research/perf-M6.md`), not confirmed (AKS-82). "Engine licences were never checked against AGPL-3.0": every M6 dependency is recorded with its licence (AKS-110, AKS-113), AKS-83 deferred by the owner. As at M1 to M5, the guide's table is the owner's verbatim text and is not edited.
  - Spec versions (guide rule 6). M6 closes with changes to spec 1 and spec 3: spec 1 goes from 0.8 to 0.9 (the "Since version 0.8" sub-heading becomes "2026-11-07, version 0.9, M6 closed (AKS-114)"), and spec 3 from 0.3 to 0.4 (a new line "2026-11-07, version 0.4, M6 closed (AKS-114)" above the lines added after 0.3); the overview's version sentence follows. Spec 2, spec 0, specs 4 to 7 and the build guide are unchanged by M6 (`git diff origin/main -- docs/specs/` touches only specs 1 and 3 before this close, and the overview at it) and keep their versions.
  - `CURRENT_MILESTONE` in `tests/benchmarks/conftest.py` stays `M6`, as set at AKS-110; M6 added no benchmark.
  - `CLAUDE.md` "Current state": M6 is Done, and a row says M7 is ready to start. Only the M6 row and a new M7 row change. `tests/test_docs.py` still finds the guide unchanged.
- **Reason:** guide rules 2, 6, 8, 9 and 10; the guide's "Session procedure", "Definition of done for any change" and M6 row; the AKS-114 acceptance list.
- **Alternatives considered:** editing the known-gaps rows in the guide (the owner's verbatim text; M1 to M5 closed their rows through DECISIONS); bumping spec 2 (M6 did not change it).
- **Spec sections updated:** spec 1 and spec 3 version lines and "Changes in this version" (bumped to 0.9 and 0.4); the overview's "What is here" version sentence.
- **Dependencies added:** during M6, `fastapi>=0.162.1`, `uvicorn>=0.54.0`, `websockets>=17.4` and, for development, `httpx2>=2.16.0` (AKS-110); `python-multipart>=0.0.33`, `openpyxl>=3.1.5` and `defusedxml>=0.7.1` (AKS-113), with the licences recorded in their entries. AKS-114 added none. Installed on 2026-11-07: fastapi 0.162.1 on Python 3.13.18 and 0.162.2 on 3.11.18 (a fresh scratch venv from `pip install -e ".[dev]"`), numpy 2.5.4 and 2.4.6, scipy 1.18.1 and 1.17.1, pint 0.26.2 and 0.25.3, the others at the same versions on both.

## 2026-11-08 · M7

### MCP research notes: protocol, SDK, transport and tool design (AKS-115)

- **Decision:** the two M7 research items of spec 4 "Research before building" are `docs/research/spec-4-mcp.md` and `docs/research/spec-4-assistant-tool-design.md`, written before any MCP code. The MCP server is built on the official Python SDK, `mcp>=2.3.0`, with its low-level `Server` (hand-written input schemas advertised unchanged, results built by lcsim, so inputs, examples and error wording are spec 4's and lcsim's, not the SDK's), over stdio only, the transport every desktop assistant uses to start a local server; the SDK answers both protocol eras, the `initialize` handshake (2024-11-05 to 2025-11-25) and the current revision 2026-07-28. The tool design rules of the second note apply: few distinct tools following the workflow, narrowing before wide (`get_schema` by type), unambiguous input names, every action answering with its consequence, errors as tool results worded for the model, descriptions with one worked example, and the AKS-116 trial measuring tool errors. Spec 4 is not contradicted; its summary is corrected (below).
- **Reason:** guide rule 3; spec 4 "Research before building" (M7 items) and "Behaviour an assistant can rely on"; the AKS-131 note "For the MCP tools"; guide "Conventions" (each dependency recorded with its licence).
- **Alternatives considered:** `MCPServer` (the decorator API: input schemas from type hints, the SDK's own argument messages and "Error executing tool" text, no structured content on an error); Streamable HTTP (a second listening process with its own port, `Origin` check and authentication beside the engine service, for no Phase 1 need); the SDK at 1.x (the import paths v2 removed; 2.3.0 is what `pip install mcp` installs).
- **Spec sections updated:** none for the notes themselves; the build changes are in the next entry.
- **Dependencies added:** `mcp>=2.3.0` (2.3.0, uploaded 2026-10-02), MIT. The packages it pulls in that lcsim did not already depend on: `mcp-types` 2.3.0, MIT; `PyJWT` 2.15.1, MIT; `cryptography` 50.0.2, Apache-2.0 OR BSD-3-Clause; `cffi` 2.1.1, MIT-0 (MIT No Attribution); `pycparser` 3.12, BSD-3-Clause; `sse-starlette` 3.7.0, BSD-3-Clause; on Windows only, `pywin32` 311, PSF. How checked: each installed distribution's metadata (`importlib.metadata`: `License` or `License-Expression`, classifiers) and the head of its licence file in `*.dist-info/licenses/` were read on 2026-11-08 in the repository's venv on Python 3.13.18 and a fresh scratch venv on 3.11.18 (`pip install "mcp==2.3.0"`, the same versions on both); `pywin32`, not installable on Linux, from its PyPI metadata only. Windows: every one is pure Python or has Windows wheels (cryptography `cp311-abi3-win_amd64`, cffi `win32`, `win_amd64` and `win_arm64` for 3.11 to 3.13; PyPI file lists read 2026-11-08). `httpx2`, a development dependency since AKS-110, now also arrives at run time through `mcp` (BSD-3-Clause, recorded in the AKS-110 entry); lcsim itself does not import it outside the tests. The others `mcp` requires (anyio, jsonschema, opentelemetry-api, pydantic, python-multipart, starlette, typing-extensions, typing-inspection, uvicorn) were already dependencies. All permissive and compatible with AGPL-3.0 for a combined work; the engine licence rule stays deferred (AKS-83). mypy needs no override: `mcp` and `mcp-types` ship `py.typed`.

### MCP server, stdio transport, service client and the six read tools (AKS-115)

- **Decision:**
  - **Layout** (`src/lcsim/mcp/`, AKS-115's Agent notes): `client.py`, the one way into the service; `tools.py`, the one registry of tools (`ToolSpec`, `TOOLS`), the one argument check (`check_arguments`) and the one way from an answer to a tool result (`run_tool`, `result_of`); `server.py`, the low-level SDK server (`build_server`), the stdio loop (`serve_stdio`) and `main`; `__main__.py`, guarded by `if __name__ == "__main__":` as the service's is, since the service and its runs use the `spawn` start method. AKS-134 adds its five tools to `TOOLS` and any input kinds they need to `KINDS`.
  - **Program** (spec 4 "How the server runs and answers", rule 5, owner to confirm). `python -m lcsim.mcp [--config-dir ...] [--data-dir ...]` serves stdio; the folders default to spec 3's per-user ones (`lcsim.service.config.default_config_dir` and `default_data_dir`, M6's path rules, made canonical with `lcsim.service.paths.canonical`) and are passed to the service when the MCP server starts it. The service is not started until a tool needs it.
  - **One way in.** Every tool call is a request to `/v1` (the client refuses any other path) on `127.0.0.1` with `Authorization: Bearer` and `X-Client: mcp`, through `urllib.request` with no proxy and redirects not followed, as the service's own probe sends its request; the body is read with `lcsim.strict_json.read_json`. The MCP server imports no service internals to do an endpoint's work: from `lcsim.service` it uses only the discovery rules (`read_service_file`, `probe`), the folder and path rules and two constants of `lcsim.service.main` (`LOCAL_ADDRESS`, `STARTUP_WAIT_S`).
  - **Starting the service on demand** (spec 3 "Lifetime"; rule 5, owner to confirm). `service.json` and the spec 3 probe decide whether a service answers; when none does, the client runs `python -m lcsim.service --config-dir <canonical> --data-dir <canonical>` with the interpreter that runs the MCP server, its standard streams on the null device (so nothing it prints can reach the MCP stdio stream; the SDK also points fd 1 at stderr while serving), detached (a new session on POSIX; `DETACHED_PROCESS | CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP` on Windows) so the service and its runs outlive the MCP server, and waits up to 30 s (`START_TIMEOUT_S`, three times the 10 s a second service start waits for the first) for `service.json` to name a service that answers. A start that exits with status 0 deferred to a service already starting, and the client keeps looking for it; any other exit, or a command that cannot run, is E193. Two clients starting at once give one service, by the service's own `service.lock` (tested). The service found is kept for later requests.
  - **Repeats** (rule 5, owner to confirm). A request is sent once more, after finding or starting the service again, only when its connection was refused (nothing reached the service) or it was answered 401 (the token check runs before any route, so nothing was applied; a service restarted since has a new token). A dropped connection, a timeout (60 s, `REQUEST_TIMEOUT_S`) or a failure while sending is E193 and never repeated, since the request may have reached a route; this matters for the write tools of AKS-134. The body of an error status is read the same way as a success's, so a body cut short or stalled is E193 too, never an exception out of the tool (review of PR #39).
  - **Results** (spec 4 "Errors are data"; rule 5, owner to confirm). A result's one text block is the body as JSON, and the same object is its `structuredContent` when the body is a JSON object (`structuredContent` must be an object, and the list endpoints return arrays; the MCP specification asks for the JSON in a text block beside it). A status of 400 or above is marked `isError` with the service's body unchanged (`{error: {code, message}, issues, ...}`), so a 404 with E185 and empty `issues` reaches the assistant with its code and message as the service wrote them. Validation issues from `validate_model` are an answer, not an error.
  - **Inputs** (rule 5, owner to confirm). Each tool's input schema lists exactly spec 4's inputs with `additionalProperties: false`; the low-level server does not apply it, so `check_arguments` does: an unknown input (in name order), then a required input missing or one of the wrong kind (in declaration order; text holding a lone surrogate, which a JSON escape can carry but no path or result can hold, is of the wrong kind, and an unknown name holding one is shown escaped), each one E184 issue at `/<input>` (the service's code for a malformed request, its `path` into the arguments as the service's points into a body), in a result marked `isError` with `error.code` E184, and no request sent. Inputs placed in a path are percent-encoded as one segment (`quote(value, safe="")`), so `..` or `/` cannot reach another route; the service then answers 404 with E185. An unknown tool name is the only protocol error (`INVALID_PARAMS`), as the MCP specification lists unknown tools.
  - **No service: E193** (new spec 1 code, the next free number after E192; pass `mcp`; rule 5, owner to confirm). One issue, `component` `null`, `path` `""`, for a service that cannot be started, does not answer, or sends a body the strict reader refuses; its hint says to check the installation and start `python -m lcsim.service`. It has no fixture: `tests/model/test_issues.py` and `tests/test_docs.py` require fixtures for schema and meaning codes only, and E193 is tested in `tests/mcp`.
  - **Outputs as the endpoints give them** (spec 4 changed first). Spec 4's "MCP tools" table said each tool "returns its response" but gave `list_projects` as `{project_id, name}`, `get_model` as "The model file" and `validate_model` as `{issues}`, while spec 3's endpoints return `{id, name, created, layout_version, model_revision, path, available}`, `{model, revision}` and `{revision, issues}`. The simplest reading keeps one rule (the tool returns its endpoint's response) and changes the table; the revision is what `edit_model`'s `base_revision` needs.
  - **Descriptions.** Each is a summary naming the endpoint, what comes back and when to use it, then one `Example:` with arguments that pass the tool's own check and an abridged result taken from the service's real answers. `get_schema`'s also states spec 2's behaviour defaults (server queue unlimited and first in first out; a blocked source discards; a full queue blocks by default; failure is preempt-resume; capacity 0 outside listed shifts; a pooled server takes its own unit first), which the component type endpoint does not give (research note, rule 6). The server's `instructions` tell the assistant how error results are laid out.
  - **Tests** (`tests/mcp/`, 71 tests): the client against a stub service (headers, no proxy, no redirect, the strict reader, repeats, timeouts and dropped connections) and real services (started on demand, a stale `service.json`, two clients at once, start failures, the start timeout); the tools through the SDK's in-memory `Client` in both eras against the M6 application served in process, each tool's method and path checked against spec 4's table read when the test runs; `main` and the stdio loop in process (its input stays open until every request has its answer, as a client's does: the SDK cancels the requests in flight when its input ends, answering them "Connection closed", and a test that ended the input at once lost the second answer on CI's Python 3.11, CI on PR #39); and `python -m lcsim.mcp` as a real process through the SDK's stdio client, starting the service on demand and leaving it answering after the MCP server has gone. The folder has no `__init__.py` or `conftest.py` (a package `mcp` would shadow the SDK, and a second top-level `conftest` would clash with `tests/engines/process`); `[tool.ruff.lint.isort]` names `mcp` third-party, since `src = ["src", "tests"]` made isort take it for first-party.
  - `CURRENT_MILESTONE` in `tests/benchmarks/conftest.py` is `M7`; M7 adds no benchmark.
  - **Found, not fixed (M6 service, for the owner):** on SIGTERM or SIGINT, uvicorn 0.54 shuts the service down gracefully (the application's lifespan ends) and then raises the signal again (`uvicorn.Server.capture_signals`), which ends the process before `lcsim.service.main._serve` removes `service.json`, so the file stays behind, against spec 3 "Discovery" ("removed when the service exits"). The MCP server copes (the stale file's probe fails and a new start overwrites it), and the tests stop services without asserting the removal. A fix belongs in `lcsim.service.main` with a test in `tests/service/test_main.py`. Candidate bucket: Phase 1a.
- **Reason:** spec 4 "MCP server", "MCP tools", "Behaviour an assistant can rely on"; spec 3 "Service rules" (Discovery, One service per user, Lifetime, Client header) and "Status codes and errors"; spec 1 "Validation issues"; guide rules 4 to 7, "Conventions" (errors as issue objects with a code; platform-neutral code) and the M7 row; the AKS-115 acceptance list and Agent notes.
- **Alternatives considered:** reusing E184 or E187 for a missing service (E184 is the client's fault and E187 the service's internal failure; neither says the service is absent, and the assistant's remedy differs); a new code for input problems (E184 already means "a field missing, unknown or of the wrong kind"); repeating every failed request once (a write that reached a route would be applied twice); `httpx2` for the client (a direct run-time dependency where `urllib.request`, which the service's probe uses, suffices; tools run in a worker thread, so a blocking client does not block the stdio loop); starting the service when the MCP server starts (spec 3 says on demand, and an assistant that never calls a tool should start nothing); returning `{project_id, name}` from `list_projects` by renaming fields (a second shape for one endpoint, and the tool would no longer return its response).
- **Spec sections updated:** spec 4 "MCP server" (the MCP server exposes the tools; `list_projects` and `get_model` in the summary table), "MCP tools" (the outputs of `list_templates`, `list_projects`, `get_model` and `validate_model`; new "How the server runs and answers"), "Changes in this version" (a dated AKS-115 line); spec 1 "Validation issues" (E193; the sentence on run and service codes) and "Changes in this version". Version numbers are not bumped: the M7 close (AKS-116) bumps them.
- **Dependencies added:** `mcp>=2.3.0` and what it pulls in, as in the entry above.

### MCP write and run tools, run limit and project-folder confinement (AKS-134)

- **Decision:**
  - **Layout.** The five tools (`create_model`, `edit_model`, `run_model`, `get_run_status`, `get_results`) are `ToolSpec`s in `src/lcsim/mcp/tools.py`, in `TOOLS` in the order of spec 4's table, which `tools/list` gives; `KINDS` gains `whole number`, `texts` (an array of text) and `edits`, and each kind now carries the JSON Schema fragment its input schema declares (`{"type": "array", "items": {"type": "object"}}` for edits). The run limit is `src/lcsim/mcp/limits.py` (`StartedRuns`, `RunLimitReached`, `RUN_LIMIT`, `MAX_UNFINISHED_RUNS`, `UNFINISHED_STATES`, and `segment`, the one percent-encoding of a path segment, moved there from `tools.py` so both use it); each `ServiceClient` holds one `StartedRuns` as `started_runs`, since one client is one MCP server's way in. `run_tool` turns `RunLimitReached` into a result as it does `ServiceUnavailable`.
  - **`create_model`** (spec 4 changed first; rule 5, owner to confirm). Inputs `name`, optional `template_id` (sent as `template`) and optional `domain`: no edit changes a model's domain (spec 3 "Model edits", Domain), so without `domain` an assistant could not start an optimization model at all. It takes no `path`, so a new project's folder is under the user data folder (spec 3), and `POST /v1/projects/open` stays out (spec 3: not an MCP tool). The output is the endpoint's answer, the project as `list_projects` lists it (`id` is the `project_id`), not spec 4's `{project_id, model}`: no endpoint returns that, composing it would take a second request whose failure would leave a project made behind an error result, and AKS-115 kept the one rule that a tool returns its endpoint's response. The description says what the empty model holds (no components, experiment `base` of 8 h, warm-up 0 s, 30 replications).
  - **`edit_model`** sends `{edits, base_revision?}`. `base_revision` must be a JSON integer (`true`, `3.0` and `"3"` are E184 at `/base_revision`); `edits` must be an array whose numbers are all finite, nesting at most 100 levels (`MAX_DEPTH`), walked without recursion: the client encodes bodies with `allow_nan=False`, so `NaN` or an infinity would otherwise fail inside the tool, and no model nests beyond six levels. Everything else about an edit (its shape, a lone surrogate in its text, which the client sends escaped) is the service's to refuse, at `/edits/<n>/<field>`. The refusals reach the assistant unchanged: E180 at `/edits/<n>`, E181 with the current `model` and `revision`, E184, E185, E186 (tested).
  - **`run_model`** sends `{experiment_id}` or `{}` and never a `target`, so a run uses the file's `run.target` (`local` in Phase 1). A search experiment runs through it as a plain one does (tested: the coffee shop with `tills` 1 to 2 finds 2), and an optimization model without `experiment_id` (tested on the product mix, objective 2,200). A model with errors is the service's 422 with its issues.
  - **`get_run_status`** returns the whole run as `GET /v1/runs/{rid}` gives it (spec 4 changed: its `{state, fraction, message}` are three of the run's fields).
  - **`get_results`** asks for the summary, then each named table once (`dict.fromkeys`, in the order first named) with `?format=json`, each name percent-encoded so `a?b` cannot add a query; the first answer with a status of 400 or above is returned unchanged and nothing after it is asked (409 E188 for a run not `done`, 404 E185 for an unknown table or an optimization run's tables); otherwise the summary's answer with `tables` added, an object keyed by name. A summary answer that is not an object (never sent by the service) is passed on as it came.
  - **The run limit: E194** (spec 4 "Limits", spec 1; new code, the next free number after E193, pass `mcp`; rule 5, owner to confirm). `StartedRuns` keeps, per `project_id` as given to `run_model`, the `run_id` of every 202 this MCP server received, in memory from its start; runs the interface, scripts or another MCP server started do not count (tested), nor do runs of an earlier MCP server process. Before each start it asks the service for each kept run: unfinished while the service says `queued` or `running` (spec 1's job states, read, never guessed); a run the service says has ended, or no longer finds (404), is forgotten, since it cannot become unfinished again; any other refusal of the check is the result and nothing is started, the run whose state is unknown still counted; no service is E193. With two unfinished, the result is marked `isError` with `{error: {code: "E194", message}, issues, run_ids}`: one issue at `/project_id` whose message names the runs and whose hint says to wait for one to end (`get_run_status`), and `run_ids` so the assistant can follow them; no request is sent to start a run. One lock covers the checks and the start, so two calls at once cannot both pass (tested with a stub whose status answers take 0.3 s). The limit is the MCP server's, as spec 4 says ("The MCP server itself"), not the service's queue.
  - **Run limits** (spec 4 "Limits"). The 10 minutes and half of the memory are the service's `run_time_limit_s` and `run_memory_limit_bytes` (spec 3 Run queue, built at M6), applied to every run whoever starts it (E192); the MCP server sets none of its own, and the `run_model` description says so.
  - **Confinement** (spec 4 "Limits" and acceptance table, M7; rule 5, owner to confirm). The simplest reading of "the MCP server reads and writes only inside project folders" that the code can meet and a test can show: no tool reads or writes a file at all; every tool is requests to the service, which does the file work in the project folder. The MCP server's own read of `service.json` in the configuration folder is discovery (spec 3), how it finds the service at all, not a tool's read; the null device that the SDK's stdio transport opens read-only for standard input is the transport's, no file in a folder. `tests/mcp/test_mcp_confinement.py` runs the server's `main` as its own process (`python -I -B -c ...`: no working folder or `PYTHON*` variable on `sys.path`, no `.pyc` written) with a PEP 578 audit hook, installed before `lcsim.mcp` is imported, that logs every `open`, folder listing, file or folder change and program start to a log opened beforehand; a scripted client calls all eleven tools, a run to `done` and refusals whose inputs name places outside the project folder (`path`, `..` in ids, a table `../manifest`) against a service in the test's process. The log holds the interpreter's module reads (under its `sys.path`), the null device and `service.json`, nothing written and no program started. Checked that the test can fail: a `get_model` changed to create a file under the data folder failed it with that file named (2026-11-08, then reverted).
  - **No data import tool** (spec 4 "Limits"; M6 "Left"). An assistant has no file to send; spec 4 now names spec 3's upload limits (64 MiB plus 64 KiB per request, 64 MiB per file, 512 MiB unpacked for `.xlsx`, 1,048,576 records, 10,000,000 cells; E189) and says files come through the interface or `POST .../data`.
  - **Worked examples.** `edit_model`'s example is the AKS-131 case: two servers in series sharing a `resource_pool` of two staff, a weekly `schedule` (period 168 h) with Monday's day shift and the night from Sunday 22:00 to Monday 06:00 split at the period end into 166 h to 168 h and 0 h to 6 h; a test applies it to a new project and finds no issues from the edit or from `validate_model`. `create_model`'s, `run_model`'s and `get_run_status`'s example results are complete JSON whose keys a test compares with the service's real answers. `get_results`' description says how `half_width` is made (t × sd / √n, t the Student quantile at (1 + confidence) / 2 for n − 1 degrees of freedom), which the AKS-116 pass rule needs to recover a standard error.
  - **Tests** (`tests/mcp/`: 110 tests, 39 of them new): `test_mcp_write_tools.py` (the request each tool sends, against a stub; input problems as E184 with nothing sent; every refusal as data against the in-process M6 application; a run to results, repeated with the same seed to the same summary; the search and optimization runs; the limit against the real service with long runs, cancelled at the end, and against a stub for the forgetting, refusal and concurrency rules) and `test_mcp_confinement.py`. `test_the_server_lists_the_six_read_tools` became `test_the_server_lists_the_eleven_tools_in_the_order_of_spec_4`; `run`, `payload`, `spec_4_tool_rows` and `NO_START` moved to `tests/mcp/mcp_support.py`, which the stub's new `respond` hook joins.
- **Reason:** spec 4 "MCP tools", "Behaviour an assistant can rely on", "How the server runs and answers", "Limits" and the M7 acceptance row; spec 3 "Endpoints" (the notes on `POST /v1/projects`, `.../model/edits`, `.../runs`, the run object, `.../summary` and `.../tables`), "Model edits" and "Status codes and errors"; spec 1 "Validation issues" and "Run targets"; the AKS-134 acceptance list and Agent notes; guide rules 4 to 7.
- **Alternatives considered:** counting the project's runs whose manifest says `client` `mcp` (would count other MCP servers' runs and earlier sessions, where spec 4 says runs "that it started"); a limit in the service (spec 4 puts it in the MCP server); reusing E188 (a run's state refusing an action) or E184 for the limit (neither says the cause, and the remedy, waiting, differs); a made-up HTTP status inside the MCP server for the refusal (it never came from the service, so it is an exception turned into a result instead); composing `{project_id, model}` for `create_model` (above); a `target` input for `run_model` (spec 4 does not name one, and only `local` exists in Phase 1); a confinement test that patches `open` in process (misses `os` and C-level opens, and shares a process with the service, whose file work is legitimate); a recursive finiteness check (fails on deep input instead of refusing it).
- **Spec sections updated:** spec 4 "MCP tools" (`create_model` inputs and output, `run_model` input cell, `get_run_status` and `get_results` outputs), "How the server runs and answers" (kinds; requests made), "Limits" (how the run limit is checked, E194, the run limits, confinement and its test, no data import tool with spec 3's upload limits), the acceptance table's evidence, "Changes in this version"; spec 1 "Validation issues" (E194; the sentence on codes the MCP server raises) and "Changes in this version". Version numbers are not bumped: the M7 close (AKS-116) bumps them.
- **Dependencies added:** none.

### Scripted coffee-shop case, real-assistant trial and the description changes (AKS-116)

- **Decision:**
  - **Level 1** (spec 4 "Assistant acceptance case"). `tests/mcp/test_coffee_shop_scripted.py` runs `python -m lcsim.mcp` as its own process through the MCP SDK's stdio client (`mcp.Client` with `StdioServerParameters`), with temporary `--config-dir` and `--data-dir`, a temporary `HOME`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` and `XDG_DATA_HOME` and a temporary working folder, so the MCP server starts its own engine service and no per-user folder can be reached. The scripted client calls `create_model`, `edit_model` (`base_revision` 0; the source, the till, the sink, two connections and `set_experiment` `base` with `length` 210 h, `warmup` 10 h, 30 replications and `seed` 12345), `validate_model`, `get_model`, `run_model`, `get_run_status` every 0.2 s until the state is final, and `get_results`. `tests/mcp/coffee_shop_description.txt` holds spec 4's text, and a test keeps it equal to the quotation in spec 4. The service is stopped at the end with every process it started (`mcp_support.stop_started`, moved from `test_mcp_server.py` and made to wait until the service and its descendants, read from `/proc`, have ended); the test asserts that the temporary home is still empty, that the per-user `service.json` is as it was before, and that the project and its run folder are in the test's data folder.
  - **`length` 210 h** (rule 5, owner to confirm). The handoff from AKS-134 named `length` 200 h, as spec 1's example `coffee_shop.json` has. An experiment's `length` includes its warm-up (spec 1 "Experiments": `warmup` less than `length`; spec 2 "Statistic definitions": the observed period starts at the end of warm-up), so 200 h measures 190 h, while the description asks to "simulate 200 hours after a 10-hour warm-up". The simplest faithful reading is `length` 210 h; spec 4's Level 1 now says so. Both lengths pass the criteria.
  - **The pass criteria, independent of ids** (rule 5, owner to confirm): `mcp_support.coffee_shop_verdict` finds the model's one `server` by type and the summary's one scenario by position; "validates with zero errors" counts issues of severity `error` from `validate_model` (warnings allowed); the run's state is the last `get_run_status`'s; each metric's standard error is `half_width / t_quantile(summary.confidence, n)` (the summary's own quantile, `lcsim.runs.statistics.t_quantile`), and both deviations must be at most 3 standard errors. A second test shows that each criterion can fail it (a deviation of 3.1, a validation error, a failed run, two servers, a missing metric, no summary), and that a warning does not.
  - **Measured** (2026-11-08, 4 cores, Python 3.13.18): seed 12345 gives `till.wait.mean` 267.74 s, half-width 9.05 s, standard error 4.43 s, −0.51 standard errors from 270 s, and `till.utilization` 0.7482, half-width 0.0044, standard error 0.0022, −0.86 standard errors from 0.75; the same on three repeats. The test's call takes 5.3 to 5.6 s, the module 7 s; a run of this size took 2.4 s in the trial (its `started` to `ended`), the rest being the starts of the MCP server and the service.
  - **Level 2** (spec 4; the acceptance row "owner, or agent if an MCP-capable assistant is available"). The build container has Claude Code CLI 2.1.319, which ran headless here (`claude -p`, `stream-json`), with model `claude-opus-5-5` (Claude Opus 5.5) as its `init` event reports, restricted to the eleven lcsim tools (`--tools ""`, `--strict-mcp-config`, `--allowedTools mcp__lcsim`, `--permission-mode dontAsk`), in a folder outside the repository and without the variables that would add the repository's `CLAUDE.md`. It completed the case on the first attempt, and again after the change below; the criteria were checked from the server's own answers with `coffee_shop_verdict`. Transcripts and findings: `docs/research/spec-4-mcp-trial-2026-11-08.md`. The row is closed by the agent. It is the same model as the build session, so the trial is not independent of the builder; a trial by the owner with another assistant is offered under "Awaiting owner", not required.
  - **Tool descriptions changed by the trial** (Build guide, known gaps, M7: "revise tool names, inputs and descriptions from what it gets wrong"; spec 4 "How the server runs and answers"; tests first, `tests/mcp/test_mcp_write_tools.py`). The assistant first set `length` 200 h for "200 hours after a 10-hour warm-up" and found from the arrival count that 190 h had been measured: `create_model` and `edit_model` now say that `length` includes the warm-up, `edit_model` with the arithmetic (200 h measured after a 10 h warm-up is `length` 210 h and `warmup` 10 h); with them the second trial set 210 h at once. It never called `validate_model`, reading `edit_model`'s issues as the whole check, which the description had called "validation's": `edit_model` now says its issues are the checks made on loading the model and that `validate_model` adds the engine's own check (spec 3: a write's issues are loading's). The CLI ran with `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_MCP_DESCRIPTION_LENGTH` 4096, and each description ends with its worked example, so a test keeps every description within 4,096 characters (`edit_model` is the longest, 3,915; it was 3,707). No tool name or input changed: the assistant made no call the server refused.
- **Reason:** spec 4 "Assistant acceptance case" and acceptance table; spec 0 "Risks" (the real-assistant transcript is an owner check, not a build gate, unless an assistant is available); guide rules 4 to 6 and the known-gaps row "The MCP tools have never run"; the AKS-116 acceptance list and Agent notes.
- **Alternatives considered:** `length` 200 h as handed off (measures 190 h, against the description); counting warnings as validation failures (spec 4 says "zero errors"); asserting fixed ids in the verdict (spec 4: "independent of ids"); calling the tools in process instead of over stdio (the ticket asks for the official client over stdio against the server process, as an assistant starts it); a description rule in the CLI's own terms only (a different assistant may cut sooner; 4,096 is the one limit observed); renaming `length` or adding an input such as `measured` (a model format change for spec 1, not a tool change, and the descriptions fixed the slip).
- **Spec sections updated:** spec 4 "How the server runs and answers" (what the trial changed), "Assistant acceptance case" (Level 1's `length` 210 h), "Changes in this version".
- **Dependencies added:** none.

### M7 closed (AKS-116)

- **Decision:**
  - M7 is closed. Every gate in the guide's M7 "Done when" row and every spec acceptance row tagged M7 passes on the top of the M7 stack (PRs #39, #40 and this change). Evidence is in `docs/sessions/2026-11-08-M7.md`:
    - "The scripted MCP case passes in CI": `tests/mcp/test_coffee_shop_scripted.py`, part of `bash scripts/check.sh`, which CI runs on Python 3.11 and 3.13;
    - "a real-assistant transcript meeting spec 4 is in `docs/research/`": `docs/research/spec-4-mcp-trial-2026-11-08.md` (Claude Code CLI 2.1.319, `claude-opus-5-5`, first attempt);
    - "No MCP tool reads or writes outside the project folder": `tests/mcp/test_mcp_confinement.py` (AKS-134) passes;
    - `pytest -m benchmark` 18 passed, 3,241 deselected, in 59.28 s (the 16 benchmark cases, M2, M4 and M5, and the two machine-count tests; M7 added none);
    - `bash scripts/check.sh` exits 0 on Python 3.13.18 and 3.11.18 with 3,233 passed and 26 deselected (16 `benchmark_coverage`, 10 `perf`), `mypy --strict` no issues in 199 source files, ruff format and ruff check clean, and line coverage of `src/lcsim` 100% (8,493 statements); `tests/test_docs.py` passes.
  - The M7 research items have their notes: spec 4 "M7 · MCP" (`docs/research/spec-4-mcp.md`) and "M7 · Assistant tool design" (`docs/research/spec-4-assistant-tool-design.md`), both AKS-115.
  - The known-gaps row assigned to M7, "The MCP tools have never run", has its outcome: they have run against a scripted client and a real assistant, and the descriptions were revised from what the assistant got wrong (entry above).
