## 2026-11-08 · M8

### Findings wording research note (AKS-117)

- **Decision:** `docs/research/spec-4-findings-wording.md` answers spec 4's M8 research item ("Findings wording") before any rule code (guide rule 3). Vendor and teaching sources (Simio's textbook, AnyLogic's resource functions, Simul8's help on accuracy and trials, Kelton et al.'s *Simulation with Arena* chapter 6 slides, Rossetti's Arena book, practitioner and teaching pages on bottlenecks and stability) support spec 4's five patterns, so spec 4 was not changed before the code. The note's six decisions are the wording readings in the entry below. No source gives 85% as a published cut-off; the thresholds stay proposals.
- **Reason:** spec 4 "Research before building"; guide rules 3 and 5.
- **Alternatives considered:** plain-language metric names in the text ("the average wait at weld") in place of metric ids (the results wireframe and the canvas use the ids, and `docs/design/canvas-interactions.md` says ids stay what findings and metrics use); a qualifier-free "busy X% of the time" for a server with a schedule or failure (Simio's textbook shows a reader taking it as a share of the whole day).
- **Spec sections updated:** none for the note itself (no finding contradicts spec 4).
- **Dependencies added:** none.

### Finding rules, their fixtures and the spec 4 corrections (AKS-117)

- **Decision:**
  - `lcsim.findings` (`src/lcsim/findings/rules.py`, `display.py`): `find(summary, model, thresholds)` reads a finished run's `summary.json` and the model that ran (the run folder's `model.json`, loaded) and returns the findings of spec 4's five rules as `Finding` objects `{rule, severity, component, scenario, text, evidence}`. It is a pure function: no run, file, network, solver, model or random number; same input, same findings. `Thresholds` holds spec 4's defaults (0.85, 0.30, 10%, 60 s, 1 per hour). `n_required` is spec 4's formula. The service's summary route and `get_results` still return `findings: []`; wiring them and making the thresholds user-adjustable is AKS-135.
  - Fixtures (`tests/findings/fixtures/`): each is a finished run, `{about, model, summary}`, the run folder's `model.json` and `summary.json`, made by `tests/findings/make_fixtures.py` on the `local` run target from an example or benchmark file at its own seed, so the tests read files and run nothing (103 tests, about 1.3 s). Regenerating writes byte-identical files (checked on 2026-11-08). `bottleneck_fires` and `not_enough_runs_fires` are `examples/three_machine_line.json` unchanged; the five `_silent` files are `examples/coffee_shop.json` unchanged; `unstable_fires` is the coffee shop with customers every 1.4 min (W201); `idle_capacity_fires` the coffee shop with `tills` 3; `optimization_result_fires` is `benchmarks/machine_count/model.json` (a search run); `optimization_result_program` is `benchmarks/product_mix/model.json`; `not_enough_runs_scenarios` is the coffee shop with the service mean as a parameter in two scenarios, 1.5 and 1.51 min, 21 h, 10 replications. Cases no example exercises (ties, a schedule or failure, a pool, an unseparated pair, search and solver variants, missing values) are made in the tests by editing a copy of a fixture, each test saying how.
  - **Measured** (2026-11-08, seed 12345, each example's own experiment; the fixtures hold these values and `tests/findings/test_rules.py` pins them): three-machine line (30 replications of 100 h with 20 h of warm-up): weld utilization 0.901 (half-width 0.011), weld mean wait 941.5 s = 15.7 min plus or minus 125.5 s = 2.09 min, 13.3% of the mean; cut 0.600 and paint 0.495; cycle time 24.5 min plus or minus 8.6%. `bottleneck` fires for weld and `not_enough_runs` for `weld.wait.mean` (n_required 60). Coffee shop (30 replications of 200 h with 10 h of warm-up): till utilization 0.749 (half-width 0.0046), till mean wait 267.6 s = 4.46 min plus or minus 9.79 s, 3.7% of the mean; cycle time plus or minus 2.8%, throughput plus or minus 0.5%. Neither rule fires. These equal the M2 measurements of spec 2's reference table (weld 0.901 and 13.3%). Spec 4's 4.8% for the coffee shop is corrected to 3.7%; M7's 3.4% was the 210 h run of the scripted MCP case. Spec 4's "original three-machine-line check (0.90, half-width 26%)" was the prototype's, which was not retained; it is replaced by the measured 0.901 and 13.3%. Owner to confirm.
  - **Readings of spec 4 "Finding rules"** (rule 5, each owner to confirm):
    1. **Input.** The rules read `summary.json` and the loaded model, nothing else; every number is the summary's. The model gives the component types, schedules, failures and pools, the objectives and constraints (key metrics, a search's sense) and the parameter types (a search's settings).
    2. **Per scenario.** `bottleneck`, `idle_capacity` and the first form of `not_enough_runs` are judged in each scenario of the summary separately and carry its id in `scenario`.
    3. **`bottleneck`.** Servers only; the highest utilization, ties by the longest mean wait (an undefined wait counting as the shortest), then the first in the model; fires at or above the threshold. When a `schedule` on the server or its pool, or a `failure` on the server, changes its capacity, the text says "busy 90% of the time it was available" (research note decision 1). With no measured wait the sentence ends after "of the time". Evidence: the server's `utilization` and `wait.mean`. It fires alongside `unstable` for an unstable server.
    4. **`unstable`.** One finding per server named by a W201 issue in `summary.json` `warnings`, each server once, in the warnings' order; `scenario` `null`, since W201 is checked across the parameter values and scenarios (spec 1) and is a warning of the run. Evidence: `warning` W201, the issue's `path` and `message`. Nothing is computed.
    5. **`idle_capacity`.** Servers only, as spec 4's detailed table says (its summary row said "server or pool"; changed to match); "has no `schedule`" read as: no schedule targets the server or the pool it names. A server with a `failure` is judged, its text "idle 75% of the time it was available". Fires strictly below the threshold. Evidence: its `utilization`.
    6. **Key metrics.** In this order, each once and only those in the summary: objective metrics, constraint metrics, each sink's `throughput` and `cycle_time.mean` (sinks in model order), and the `wait.mean` of the server `bottleneck` would pick, whatever its utilization.
    7. **The floor.** A time (unit `s`) is judged when its mean is at least 60 s and a rate (`1/s`) at least 1 per hour, both at or above; a key metric in another unit (a utilization named in a constraint) has no floor beyond a mean that is not zero. A metric with an undefined mean or half-width is not judged.
    8. **One finding, the widest metric, the largest n.** The finding lists every key metric whose half-width is above the share of its mean, in key-metric order; `{metric}`, `{mean}` and `{half_width}` are those of the listed metric whose half-width is the largest share of its mean; `{n_required}` is the largest of the listed metrics' n_required (Kelton et al.: "Use max of sample sizes"). The interval named is the threshold's ("for a 10% interval"; "2.5%" when the threshold is 0.025). Evidence: each listed metric's summary entry and `n_required`.
    9. **n_required.** Computed exactly, each number read as the decimal `summary.json` writes it (the shortest repr) and the threshold as the decimal written, so 10 × (0.2 / 0.1)² is 40, not just above it and so 50; rounded up to the next multiple of 10; with the threshold changed, its share replaces 0.1.
    10. **Two scenarios.** For each entry of `differences` (each pair, in the summary's order), the key metrics of both scenarios, the earlier's first; a metric fires when its difference is not `separated`, its half-width is defined and above the share of the smaller of the two scenario means, and that smaller mean passes the floor of reading 7. One finding per pair: `scenario` is the later scenario (the difference's `scenario`), the text "{earlier} and {later} cannot be told apart on {metrics} yet." with the metrics joined, evidence `scenarios` [earlier, later] and each listed metric's difference entry. Measured: common random numbers (spec 2) separate even close scenarios (1.5 and 1.51 min of service: each wait plus or minus 26%, the paired difference 8.6 s plus or minus 4.0 s), so on the Phase 1 examples the pair form fires only when the scenarios' random numbers decouple; its firing case is an edited copy of that run.
    11. **`optimization_result`, search.** From the `search` block: "Best found: {settings}. {metric} improved from {before} to {after}." when the best is better than the baseline in the sense of the objective the experiment's `search` names (review of PR #42: two objectives may name the same metric, so the sense is not found by metric); "went from" when it is not or a mean is undefined ("not defined"); "Best found: the starting values, {settings}. No setting tried improved {metric}, which is {before}." when the best is the baseline; "Best found: {settings}. The starting values break a constraint; {metric} went from {before} to {after}." when the baseline is infeasible; "No setting tried meets every constraint. With the starting values, {settings}, {metric} is {before}." (severity warning) when `status` is `infeasible`. Spec 2 says the result states whether the best is separated from the confirmed runner-up: when another confirmed feasible candidate exists, the text adds "It is clearly better than the next best setting tried." or "The next best setting tried cannot be told apart from it yet." Settings are `name = value` in the order of `best.set`, a `quantity` parameter as a time. `scenario` is the summary's one scenario, `default`. Evidence: `status`, `best`, `metric`, `before`, `after`, `separated`.
    12. **`optimization_result`, mathematical optimization.** Spec 4's pattern has a `{before}`, which a solved program does not have; spec 4's summary row asks for the best values and the tight constraints. Optimal: "Best found: chairs = 24, tables = 14. The objective is 2,200, with wood and labour tight." (variables and constraints in model order; "; no constraint is tight." or a full stop without constraints); infeasible: "No values meet every constraint: the problem is infeasible."; unbounded: "The objective can be improved without limit: a bound or a constraint is missing." (both severity warning). `scenario` `null`. Evidence: `status`, and when optimal `objective`, `variables` and the `tight` constraint ids.
    13. **Severity.** `info` for `bottleneck`, `idle_capacity` and a found optimum; `warning` for `unstable`, `not_enough_runs` and an optimization without one.
    14. **Order.** `optimization_result`, then `unstable`, then for each scenario in the summary's order `bottleneck`, `idle_capacity` (servers in model order) and `not_enough_runs`, then the scenario pairs.
    15. **Display units** (research note decision 6). Times in the largest Phase 1 unit they reach (s, min, h, d) and rates per hour, three significant figures, no exponent, commas between thousands; a half-width in its mean's unit ("15.7 min plus or minus 2.09 min"); utilization, availability and scheduled fraction as percentages (whole percentages in `bottleneck` and `idle_capacity`, a half rounding up; three figures in `not_enough_runs`); an optimization's values to four figures ("2,200", "24").
    16. **Thresholds.** A `Thresholds` object, an input to `find`: the two utilizations from 0 to 1, the share above 0 and at most 1, the floors at least 0, all finite numbers. A value outside raises `ValueError`, an internal check: the settings of AKS-135 must check the values first and report a problem as a spec 1 issue with a code.
  - `CURRENT_MILESTONE` in `tests/benchmarks/conftest.py` is `M8`. No benchmark has milestone M8, so `pytest -m benchmark` runs the same 18.
- **Reason:** spec 4 "Finding rules", "Findings in plain language" and acceptance table (M8); spec 2 "Statistic definitions" and "Simulation-based optimization"; spec 1 "Validation issues" (W201) and "Run folder and results"; guide rules 3 to 7 and the known-gaps row "Finding thresholds are proposals" (M8 fixtures); the AKS-117 acceptance list and Agent notes; the research note.
- **Alternatives considered:** recomputing stability from the model (spec 4: one computation, not two); fixtures as model files run by the tests (slower, and the Agent notes ask for run results); hand-building every fixture (the examples give real runs for four of the five rules, and two of the others are small changes to the coffee shop); one finding per metric for `not_enough_runs` (spec 4: one per run and scenario); `n_required` from the first listed metric rather than the largest (Kelton et al. take the largest); a plain binary-float `n_required` (a boundary case such as 10 × (0.2/0.1)² gave 50); `scenario` `default` for `unstable` (W201 names a case, not a scenario); a pool as `idle_capacity` (spec 4's detailed table names servers).
- **Spec sections updated:** spec 4 warning line, "Findings in plain language" (the idle-capacity row), "Detailed specification" opening line, "Finding rules" (the measured values replacing 4.8% and the prototype's 0.90 and 26%, the example finding, "How the rules read this table"), "Changes in this version" (lines after version 0.4). Spec 4 stays at 0.4; AKS-135 bumps it when M8 closes.
- **Dependencies added:** none.

### Findings in the run summary and `get_results`, user-adjustable thresholds (AKS-135)

- **Decision:**
  - `GET /v1/runs/{rid}/summary` returns `{summary, findings, issues}` (`src/lcsim/service/run_service.py`): `summary` as `summary.json` holds it, `findings` the `Finding.as_dict()` objects of `lcsim.findings.find`, and `issues` the W220 issues of reading the thresholds. The MCP `get_results` passes them on unchanged (`{summary, findings, issues, tables}`); its description says what findings are and how to read them, and its worked example is the three-machine line with its two findings (3,397 of the 4,096 characters the trial's assistant takes). AKS-117's `find` is unchanged; no defect was found in it.
  - **Measured end to end** (2026-11-08, through the service with the product's process adapter, each example unchanged, its own experiment at seed 12345; `tests/service/test_summary_findings.py` pins them, and `tests/mcp/test_mcp_write_tools.py` runs the three-machine line through the MCP tools): three-machine line (30 replications of 100 h with 20 h of warm-up): weld utilization 0.901 (0.9009847, half-width 0.0109), weld mean wait 941.5 s = 15.7 min plus or minus 125.5 s = 2.09 min, 13.3% of the mean, n_required 60; cut 0.600, paint 0.495. `bottleneck` fires for weld ("weld is the bottleneck: busy 90% of the time, with an average wait of 15.7 min.") and `not_enough_runs` for `weld.wait.mean`. Coffee shop (30 replications of 200 h with 10 h of warm-up): till utilization 0.749 (0.7486614, half-width 0.0046), mean wait 267.6 s = 4.46 min plus or minus 9.79 s, 3.7% of the mean; no finding. These are the same numbers, to every digit printed, as AKS-117's fixtures (entry above), as expected: the service runs the same `local` target from the same file bytes and seed, and results do not depend on `workers` (spec 1 "Identical runs"). The scripted MCP case (the coffee shop as spec 4 describes it, 210 h with 10 h of warm-up, seed 12345) also fires no finding. Each example run takes about 4 s on 4 cores.
  - **Readings** (rule 5, each owner to confirm):
    1. **The model the rules read** is the run folder's `model.json` (spec 1 "Run folder and results": the file's bytes, unchanged), never the project's current `model.json`, which may have changed since the run. It is read by the strict reader and loaded with the schema pass, building and the meaning pass, as the run loaded it, except that E162 is not checked: a `data` reference is not compared with the project's data again (the files may have changed or gone since; the run has its own copies, and the rules read no data). The manifest's `seed` stands in for a seed the file leaves out (the rules read none). A `model.json` that cannot be read or loaded (changed outside the product) is 404 with E185, as a `summary.json` the strict reader refuses already is.
    2. **A summary the rules cannot read.** A `summary.json` that the strict reader takes but whose layout is not a run's (changed outside the product) makes the rules fail; that is 404 with E185 with the traceback logged, never a 500.
    3. **Response shape.** `issues` is a third key, always present and usually empty, so the W220 issues reach the user and the assistant where the findings are; `findings` and `issues` keep the spec 1 and spec 4 object layouts.
    4. **Where the thresholds live.** `finding_thresholds` in `settings.json`, next to the run settings, an object whose five keys are the fields of `Thresholds` (the two floors of `not_enough_runs`, 60 s and 1 per hour, are adjustable too, since `find` takes them as thresholds), with the ranges `Thresholds` checks.
    5. **When they are read.** At each summary request, not at service start as the run settings are: the thresholds change only how results are read, so a change takes effect at the next request without a restart; nothing is stored, so the same run, files and thresholds give the same findings.
    6. **A value that cannot be used** is refused as W220, a new spec 1 service code, at its place in `settings.json` (`/finding_thresholds/<key>`, a JSON Pointer into that file), and that key's default is used while the other keys are kept; so is a key that is not one of the five (a misspelt key would otherwise be ignored silently), `finding_thresholds` that is not an object (at `/finding_thresholds`), and a `settings.json` that exists but is not a JSON object the strict reader takes (at `""`, all defaults). A warning, not an error: nothing but the value is refused, and the summary is still answered. No existing code fits: E184 is a malformed request and E186 a project folder. Each W220 is also logged. AKS-117's `ValueError` in `Thresholds` stays an internal check that the settings never reach.
    7. **Validation fixtures.** W220 is a service code, so, as for E180 to E194, its cases are tests (`tests/service/test_finding_settings.py`, every case yielding W220 as its only issue, with `path`, a message naming the key and a hint), not a model file in `tests/model/fixtures/issues/`, which holds schema and meaning codes only.
    8. **The known-gaps row** "Finding thresholds are proposals" in the guide and `CLAUDE.md` records the M8 check, as AKS-135's acceptance list asks, although M1 to M7 left the guide's table as the owner's verbatim text; both copies stay equal (`tests/test_docs.py`).
- **Reason:** spec 3 "Endpoints" (`GET .../summary` "findings empty until M8") and "Service rules"; spec 4 "Finding rules" ("Thresholds are proposed defaults and are user-adjustable"), "MCP tools" and acceptance table; spec 1 "Validation issues" and "Run folder and results"; guide rules 5 and 6; the AKS-135 scope and acceptance list; the known-gaps row "Finding thresholds are proposals".
- **Alternatives considered:** the project's current model (wrong after an edit: `tests/service/test_summary_findings.py` replaces it with another model and the findings stay); the full meaning pass on the run's model (a run whose data file was later changed or removed would lose its summary); storing the findings with the run (a threshold change would need a rerun, and the rules are cheap); thresholds read at start like the run settings (a restart for a reading change); invalid values only logged, as the run settings are (the user would not learn that a threshold was ignored); refusing the whole summary for a bad threshold (the results would be hidden by a setting); an E code (the request does not fail); flat keys such as `finding_bottleneck_utilization` (one object keeps the five together and gives a plain pointer); a settings endpoint (spec 3 says Settings writes the keys at M9).
- **Spec sections updated:** spec 1 "Validation issues" (W220, the sentence on run and service codes) and version 1.1; spec 3 warning line, "Service rules" (Finding thresholds), the endpoint table's summary row, the `GET .../summary` note, and version 0.5; spec 4 warning line, "Detailed specification" opening line, "Finding rules" (thresholds and the end-to-end check), "How the rules read this table" (Thresholds), "MCP tools" (`get_results`), the acceptance table's M8 row, and version 0.5; the overview's version sentence; the known-gaps row in the guide and `CLAUDE.md`.
- **Dependencies added:** none.

## 2026-11-08 · M9

### Interface shell, service connection, settings endpoint, first-start notice and Home (AKS-119)

- **Decision:** M9 starts (`CURRENT_MILESTONE` is `M9` in `tests/benchmarks/conftest.py`). The interface reaches the service as AKS-119's description reads it: the service serves the built interface, so the interface's origin is the service's own; a launcher finds or starts the service and opens the interface with the token in the address's fragment; `GET` and `PUT /v1/settings` read and write `settings.json`. Built: `src/lcsim/service/interface_files.py`, `settings.py`, `settings_store.py`, the `Origin` rule in `auth.py`, `--ui-dir` in `main.py`, the launcher `src/lcsim/launcher/`, and `ui/` (the frame with the navigation rail, the API client, settings, the first-start notice, Home and placeholders for the other screens), with component tests, end-to-end tests against a real service and a CI job. The stale `service.json` after `SIGTERM` (M7 "Left") is fixed in `src/lcsim/service/main.py`. Readings under guide rule 5, each owner to confirm:
  1. **Interface files.** `GET /v1/ui/{path}` serves the folder `python -m lcsim.service --ui-dir` names, by default `ui/dist` beside `src/lcsim` (a source checkout once `npm run build` has run; an installed package has none until M11 packaging names one). These files need no token, so a browser can load the page before it has one; the `Host` check stays. `/v1/ui/` and a path ending in `/` give `index.html`; a name that is empty, `.` or `..`, holds `\`, `:` or a control character, or a file that resolves outside the folder, is refused as 404 with E185, as is a missing folder or file (no other code, so a probe learns nothing). `HEAD` is answered as `GET`; other methods need the token and are 405. Each file carries a `Content-Security-Policy` that keeps the page to the service (`default-src 'self'`, `connect-src` the service's `ws://` addresses, inline styles allowed for React's style attributes and the token style sheet, `frame-ancestors 'none'`), `Cache-Control: no-cache`, `nosniff` and `no-referrer`. Media types are fixed by suffix, not read from the platform (on Windows the registry may name JavaScript `text/plain`).
  2. **The interface's `Origin`** is `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` or `http://localhost:<port>` (the host in any letter case, the scheme exactly `http`): both are how a browser reaches the service. Any other `Origin` stays refused with E183; `ServiceConfig.allowed_origins` stays for extra origins, such as the desktop shell's at M11.
  3. **Route table.** Spec 3's table writes the row `GET /v1/ui/{path}`; FastAPI's route is `/v1/ui/{path:path}` (a path parameter that holds `/`). `lcsim.service.app.table_path` writes a parameter without its converter, for the route table and the route recorder alike.
  4. **Settings endpoint.** `GET /v1/settings` is `{settings, issues}`: the six keys (`tier`, the three run settings, `finding_thresholds`, `interface`) as the service takes them now, defaults filled in, with the W220 issues of the thresholds (the run settings' refusals stay log lines, as at M6). `PUT` takes any of the six keys; a key given replaces the stored one whole (the whole `interface` or `finding_thresholds` object, keys left out taking their defaults), a key not given is kept, and `null` removes a key so its default applies. Values are checked by the rules the service reads them with (`whole_in`, `real_in`, `THRESHOLD_RANGES`, the tier names); every problem is an E184 issue at its JSON Pointer in the body, all in one 400, and nothing is written. E184, not W220: the request is malformed, and W220 stays the code for a value read from the file. A write keeps any other key of `settings.json` and replaces the file through a temporary file and a rename, under a lock; a file that cannot be read is replaced, as the first start replaces it. The response is the new `GET` body. Partial updates were chosen so that saving one preference never writes machine-dependent defaults (the memory limit is half the physical memory) into the file.
  5. **Interface preferences** (`interface` in `settings.json`; the owner design review's Settings items): `first_start_seen` (default `false`), `ask_before_deleting` (`true`), `undo_steps` (`null`, meaning the tier's recommendation of design choice 1: 200 on `low`, 500 on `mid`, 1,000 on `high`; otherwise a whole number from 100, spec 3's minimum, to 10,000), `theme` (`system`, `light`, `dark`), `text_size` (100, 125, 150, 175, 200), `motion` (`system`, following the operating system, then `full`, `reduced`, `off`) and `colour_blind_charts` (`false`). The service stores them and reads none; a stored value that cannot be used reads as its default and is logged.
  6. **Launcher.** `python -m lcsim.launcher` reads `service.json`, probes it as a second start does, and when nothing answers (also for a stale file) starts `python -m lcsim.service` detached (a new session on Linux and macOS; `DETACHED_PROCESS | CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP` on Windows), its output appended to `service.log` in the configuration folder; it waits up to 30 seconds (six times the 5-second ready target, for a slow first start on the low tier) for `service.json` to name a service that answers, then opens `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/v1/ui/#token=<token>` in the default browser and prints the address without the token, or with `--print-url` prints the whole address (for scripts and the end-to-end tests). A start that exits with status 0 found another service starting, so waiting goes on; one that fails ends the wait at once. Exit status 1, naming the log, when nothing answers in time. Known limit: a browser started with an address holds the token in its command line for a moment, which another user of the same machine could read; the desktop shell (M11) opens its own window instead.
  7. **Token in the page.** The page takes the token from the fragment (never sent to the service, so it is in no request line or log), removes it with `history.replaceState`, and keeps it in the tab's `sessionStorage`, so reloading the tab works; another start of the service has another port, so another origin and empty storage. A page opened without a token says to use the launcher.
  8. **Stale `service.json` after a signal.** uvicorn 0.54 raises a caught `SIGTERM` or `SIGINT` again once it has shut down, which ended the process before `_serve` removed the file. `SignalServer` keeps the caught signals, `main` removes the file and releases the lock, then raises them again, so the exit status still says how the service was stopped. The launcher never takes a stale file for a running service, since the probe fails; the end-to-end harness now checks that every service it stops removes its file.
  9. **Home: recent projects** are `GET /v1/projects` newest registered first, each with its name, creation date and folder; the service keeps no last-edited time, so the wireframe's "Edited today" is not shown. A project listed as not available is shown as "Not available" and does not open.
  10. **Home: Learn and Build.** Learn moves focus to the template gallery and orders it students first ("Students", "Both", then "Business", as `flows.md` L1 orders lessons); with no templates (until M10) the gallery shows its empty state, "No templates are installed yet." Build is shown and unavailable (`aria-disabled`, with its reason as visible text and as its description) until its form is built at M10, since every Build answer opens a template.
  11. **Home: new and open.** "New empty project" asks a name and "Process (flows and queues)" or "Plan (decisions and limits)" (`domain` `process` or `optimization`). "Open a project folder" takes a typed full path, since a browser has no folder picker; the desktop shell's picker comes at M11. The interface does not check either field: the service's issues (E184, E185, E186) are shown as it wrote them, with code, message, hint and path. A created or opened project goes to the Canvas route, a placeholder whose events socket shows the model's revision, which exercises the token message and the interface's `Origin` end to end.
  12. **Screens and regions.** The screen is kept in the fragment (`#/canvas/<project id>`), so a reload stays on it; a route naming a project that is gone goes Home. The five project screens wait in the rail (`aria-disabled`, described "Open a project first.") until a project is open. Arrow keys, Home and End move in the rail; F6 and Shift+F6 cycle rail, top bar and main area (`accessibility.md` "Focus order").
  13. **First-start notice.** Focus starts on Continue (its only control); Escape does not close it. Continue closes it and saves the whole `interface` object with `first_start_seen` `true`; if that save is refused, the service's problem is shown in the top bar and the notice returns at the next start. Settings, About (a placeholder until AKS-138) repeats its text and links to `LICENSE.txt` and `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt`, which the build writes beside `index.html`.
  14. **Licence notices in the build.** A Vite plugin (`ui/plugins/notices.ts`) lists every npm package whose modules are bundled and writes each one's name, version, licence and licence text to `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt` (the OFL asks that the font licence travel with the fonts; MIT, ISC and BSD ask for their notices), and copies the repository's `LICENSE` as `LICENSE.txt`. A bundled package without a licence file fails the build.
  15. **Tooling.** Biome is the one formatter and linter for `ui/`, with its recommended rules except `noDescendingSpecificity` (a style rule that flags unrelated selectors in one style sheet) and without Markdown, so `ui/README.md` keeps the repository's table style. TypeScript is 7.0.2, the current stable release (the native compiler), run as `tsc --noEmit`.
- **Reason:** Spec 3 "Architecture", "API" (no private channel), "Service rules" (Token, Discovery, Hardening "fixed at M9", Run queue and Finding thresholds "Settings writes them at M9", First start), "Screens" 1; the AKS-119 description and its comment of 2026-11-08 (the readings 1 to 4 and the settings endpoint); `docs/design/` (version 2, `accessibility.md`, `flows.md` "Shared start", wireframes 02 and 08); M7 "Left" (the stale `service.json`); guide rules 3, 5 and 6.
- **Alternatives considered:** CORS headers for a page served elsewhere (spec 3 says none are sent, and every other origin would need a list); a static file server outside the service (a second port and origin, and a second process to find); `PATCH` with JSON merge patch for settings (the ticket names `PUT`; merging nested objects key by key makes "restore one default" and "replace the set" two different requests); whole-file `PUT` (the client would write every default, the memory limit among them, into the file); W220 for a bad `PUT` value (that code means a value read from the file); browser storage for the preferences (its origin changes with the port); the token in `localStorage` (it would outlive the tab for no gain, since the next start has another origin); a client-side check of the project name (the interface would write its own text for what the service already reports); the launcher as a module rather than a package (`python -m` with a guarded `__main__`, as the service has); `react-router` (one more dependency for seven screens, which a fragment holds).
- **Spec sections updated:** spec 3 warning line, "API" table, "Hardware tiers", "Service rules" (Token, Discovery, One service per user, new Launcher and Interface page rules, Hardening, Run queue, Finding thresholds, First start), "Detailed specification" opening line, "Endpoints" (three rows and their notes), "Status codes and errors" (404), "Changes in this version" (new paragraph "Since version 0.5, during the M9 build:", three lines); spec 1 "Validation issues" (W220 also in `GET /v1/settings`) and "Changes in this version" (new paragraph "Since version 1.1, during the M9 build:"); `docs/design/wireframes/README.md` and the new `02c-new-project.svg`; `docs/research/spec-3-framework-check.md` (dated re-check). No version number moves: the M9 close (AKS-121) does that.
- **Dependencies added:** no Python package. npm packages, pinned exactly in `ui/package.json` and `ui/package-lock.json`. How checked, 2026-11-08: version, release date and licence from the npm registry (`npm view <package>@<version> time license`), and every installed package's `package.json` licence field and the first line of its `LICENSE` file read by a script over `npm ls --all --parseable`.

All 130 installed packages are permissive or MPL-2.0: 95 MIT, 10 ISC, 10 Apache-2.0, 3 BSD-3-Clause, 2 BSD-2-Clause, 2 MIT OR Apache-2.0, 1 BlueOak-1.0.0 (`lru-cache` 11.5.3, permissive), 3 OFL-1.1 (the fonts) and 4 MPL-2.0 (`axe-core`, `@axe-core/playwright`, and Vite's CSS tool `lightningcss` 1.33.0 with its platform binary). MPL-2.0 is a file-level copyleft that section 3.3 makes compatible with the GNU licences; these four are build and test tools, never shipped in the interface, so nothing under MPL-2.0 is distributed. Three installed folders carry no licence file of their own (`@biomejs/cli-linux-x64` and `@rolldown/binding-linux-x64-gnu`, platform binaries of `@biomejs/biome` and Vite, and `saxes` 6.0.0, ISC in its `package.json`); none is shipped. What the build ships is listed in `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt`: React, React DOM, `scheduler`, Phosphor and the three fonts. Not legal advice; the release lawyer review (spec 0) confirms.

- **CI:** `.github/workflows/checks.yml` gains the job `interface`: Python 3.13 with the package installed, Node 22, `npm ci`, `npm run check`, `npm run build`, `npx playwright install --with-deps chromium`, `npm run e2e`. New action `actions/setup-node@v4` (MIT, github.com/actions), pinned to the major version as the M0 CI entry pins the others; tag `v4` checked with `git ls-remote` on 2026-11-08 (it points at v4.4.0). `actionlint` 1.7.12 reports no errors.

### Engine gap: Tauri lacks a build in the agent container

- **Decision:** The desktop shell (Tauri 2, spec 3 "Architecture") is not built at AKS-119. Tauri needs WebKitGTK on Linux, which cannot be installed in the agent container, and the agent has no Windows machine; no untested Rust is written. Workaround: the service serves the interface and the launcher opens it in the default browser; the interface is built and tested in Chromium with Playwright against the real service. The shell, its own origin (to be added to `allowed_origins`), its folder picker and `tauri-driver` are left for M11 packaging, unless the M9 close (AKS-121) can build the shell in a CI job.
- **Reason:** Spec 3 "Architecture" and "Packaging"; the framework research note's "Tested" asked for a minimal Tauri window first, which this container cannot run.
- **Alternatives considered:** Writing the Tauri configuration and Rust entry point without building them (untested code, against the owner's "Make no mistake"); Electron (the spec's fallback only if WebKitGTK proves unworkable, which is not what was found).
- **Spec sections updated:** none beyond the Launcher rule's note that the desktop shell opens its own window at M11; `docs/research/spec-3-framework-check.md` (dated line).
- **Dependencies added:** none.
